# The Book / OF METEORS.
## 31
<meta>catchword: Winds- <note>original: "Vento-"</note></meta>

Now test these earthly examples of the origin of the winds; namely, you can see that when water boils in a pot, it gives off a wind from itself. You also see further that a wind comes from all things that boil <note>original: "Ebullieren." From the Latin ebullire, meaning to bubble up or effervesce.</note>, whether they be dry or moist. A water that boils gives a warm, moist wind, as the process itself demonstrates. A dry boiling, as is known to the alchemists, gives a dry wind. Now, concerning this, know that the **Fiery Vulcan** <term>Vulcanus Igneus: The personification of the alchemical force of fire that transforms and refines matter.</term> in the stars boils the **Three Firsts** <term>Tria Prima: The three fundamental principles of Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury which Paracelsus believed composed all matter.</term> according to their nature; from this the wind arises and whatever qualities it possesses, just as the Fiery Vulcan does through wood. And the Fiery Vulcan is himself the wind, for he is placed within the Three Firsts. Where the Three Firsts are not present, there is no fire. He is likewise in the wind-stars.

<margin>
<unclear>Example</unclear>
of the
<unclear>Three</unclear>
</margin>

Regarding this, it is now to be known that the Fiery Vulcan is nothing other than a sulfur, a saltpeter <note>original: "Salniter." Also known as niter; Paracelsus viewed it as a vital aerial salt.</note>, and a mercury. Now, when the spark of fire falls into these three, it is in action and does what belongs to it, according to how the three are ordered. For the **sulfur of wood** <note>original: "Sulphur ligni"</note> is different from the **sulfur of the winds** <note>original: "Sulphur ventorum"</note>; the **saltpeter of wood** <note>original: "Salnitrum ligni"</note> is different from the **saltpeter of the winds** <note>original: "Salnitrum ventorum"</note>; the **mercury of wood** <note>original: "Mercurius ligni"</note> is different from the **mercury of the winds** <note>original: "Mercurius Ventorum"</note>. As each specific type is, so is its fire and its operation. For everything that grows does so out of these three. Therefore, it is first of all necessary to understand the **Three Firsts** <note>original: "tres Primas"</note> well, for according to their nature, they are the mother of all things.

Now you see well what a common fire does in common Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury; how much more refined <note>original: "gradiert." In alchemy, "grading" refers to intensifying or purifying a substance's properties to a higher degree.</note> is this **Prime Matter** <note>original: "Prima materia"</note> in the Firmament, stronger and more powerful. You should note that there is no other origin of the winds than when the Three Firsts are moved into operation by Vulcan; thus the Three Firsts move into their action. This action then produces the wind and its nature along with it—cold or warm, dry or moist. As was mentioned above, just as a wind goes out from a fire which it creates itself, so it is also to be understood here. For God has así set the generation of the Firmament together, and with such properties, that the three shall give and bring forth all things in their places where God has ordained them, and with their operations they go into the center of the world.

<meta>catchword: Winds</meta>

...burns / also know that Saltpeter burns / likewise Mercury / and all three have a fiery nature / until they reach their operation / that is / their Final Matter <term>Ultima Materia: In alchemy, the final or "end" state of a substance after it has been fully transformed or perfected.</term>. Now Sulfur receives the **Fiery Vulcan** <term>Vulcanus Igneus: The personification of the transformative power of fire.</term> / and because the others are embodied within it, for that reason one moves with the other / and all three turn into a Fire: This fire now cooks the Wind. Just as you visibly see from the earthly versions of these **Three Firsts** how they make their impulse <note>original: "Impetum"</note> and movements <note>original: "motus"</note> and storms <note>original: "tempeſtates"</note>.

From this it follows / that in the birthing of the winds, the stars are vials <note>original: "Violas"</note> and flasks <term>Cucurbit: A gourd-shaped chemical vessel used in a laboratory for distillation.</term> / and the Meteoric Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt are within them / according to an ethereal nature / these same ones work through the Ethereal Vulcan / in those same vials / from those ethereal workings now grow ethereal operations / which then are the winds / which are driven out of those same hollows <note>original: "Concauitatibus"</note> onto the lower globe according to the nature and severity of those same Three First Things <note>original: "rerum trium primarum"</note>.

Therefore, understand the workings of the firmament by these earthly examples / Not, however, in the same degree / but / as far as the heavens are from the earth / so much more powerful are they over the earth: And as much as the firmament stands in clarity above earthly coarseness / so much higher is its operation graded / And so much is it also changed in its three species <note>original: "tribus Speciebus"</note>. Therefore the earthly example is sufficient to understand meteorology <note>original: "Meteoricam"</note> / and is also sufficiently proven by it: Just as every day things that are not clear before our eyes / should be understood through that which is clear. Since the earthly operation is clear to us and within sufficient understanding and knowledge: Now that same thing is an example and likeness of that which is also the same / and yet we do not see it. Even Christ announced his Word in parables <note>original: "Parabolis"</note> / through which parables a greater thing shall and must be understood. Therefore I present the earthly for the heavenly / which is also perishable just like the earthly.

Now know / that the time and hour of the rising of such winds may in some part be well known through astronomy <note>original: "Astronomia"</note> / and their species understood fundamentally: Yet that same thing is not meteoric but astronomic / to which I entrust it. But now when they blow / know / that they proceed according to their parts and harmony / as much harmony <note>original: "Concordantz"</note> makes much strong wind / frequent harmony and generation makes frequent wind / many species and a strong Vulcan make great, powerful winds.

<summary>Paracelsus explains that the stars act as chemical vessels (vials and flasks) where the Ethereal Vulcan 'cooks' the Three Firsts to create wind. He argues that visible earthly processes are parables for understanding these invisible celestial operations.</summary>

# OF METEORS.
## 33
<meta>catchword: operations</meta>
<sig>I</sig>

Winds, therefore, also knock down houses and trees. For the wind is just as much physical <note>original: "Corporalisch"</note> and substantial in its nature as a wall or a stone that falls from a height. Although the stone has one kind of body <note>original: "Corpus"</note> and the wind another, there is nothing to wonder at in this: for the wind is also a body that can certainly knock over other bodies, just like a visible body. For it is not only that which is visible that is a body; rather, both the visible and invisible things that God has created are all physical, yet in two forms, invisible and visible, but with equal power. For water carries a great weight of wood, as soft as it is; it pushes great stones away in streams, even though it is thin and soft: so also know that the wind has similar force and power.

So now understand the origins of the winds: they arise only in the **Wind-stars** <term>Windsterne: In Paracelsian meteorology, these are celestial points or "stars" that act as the source or vents for the winds.</term> and thus become mature <note>original: "zeitig"</note> through a volcanic process <term>Vulcanisch: Referring to the alchemical power of fire (Vulcan) that transforms and matures substances.</term> at their appointed times, and break out in the center of the globe. With this, they attack whatever their nature and properties touch, according to their essence: just as **Boreas** <note>The North Wind</note> coagulates <note>hardens or freezes</note>, **Auster** <note>The South Wind</note> resolves <note>melts or dissolves</note>, **Eurus** <note>The East Wind</note> preserves <note>original: "conferuiert"</note>, and **Zephyrus** <note>The West Wind</note> putrefies <note>causes decay</note>. As they are now able to complete their
operations <note>original: "opera"</note>

<column-break/>

they demonstrate it in that very place, according to their nature. If it happens at times that their malice <note>original: "malitia"</note> is taken away, then they can do nothing.

Thus, as the winds now rise from their outlets <term>Emunctories: Traditionally, organs that remove waste from the body; here, the "wind-circle" is envisioned as having vents similar to a living organism.</term> in the wind-circle—like the breathing <note>original: "anhelieren"</note> of a human—their first path is over the sea. Now it is no less true that the circle does not stand so far above the sea that it merely passes over it; rather, it is so broad that it even reaches down to the lower circle of the lower globe, that is, down to the **third Peroledum** <term>Peroledum: A technical term used by Paracelsus to describe specific layers, intervals, or regions of the atmosphere or the elemental spheres.</term>, below and above. Since the upper third Peroledum also passes into the middle one, know that in this way it hits the middle Peroledum in its body just as it passes by, whether it be above or below, on whichever side—be it in the **Arctic** or **Antarctic** regions, etc. When the third Peroledum is hit, the wind enters the water and moves the water, so that it forces its way through the water upward or downward according to its impulse <note>original: "Impetus"</note>, while the other part stays on top of the water. Now the wind-part does not remain in the water, but goes out of the water into the air. Through such passage, it creates storms <note>original: "tempestates"</note> in the sea and in the water.

...forth: For it should not have gone in such a way, but rather upon the water; but as it uses its impetus <note>original: "impetu"; force or momentum</note> in the air, so it also uses it in the water, whereby it makes waves and floods in the sea, horrible and terrifying, just as upon the water: for it throws the water up, pushes it into the heights, and no one can know how strongly it lies in the water. For its greatness overcomes the water, and the water must allow it cataracts <term>Cataracten: In this context, openings or floodgates through which the wind bursts.</term> and caverns <note>original: "Cauernam"</note>, so that it may go out into its body of the elements. And not only does it always go so completely out of the water and make its caverns, but it is so subtle that it also goes through the pores <note>original: "Poras"</note> of the earth, through walls, through all barriers it goes. As is visibly known, it also goes through the mountains, through rocks, as it passes over the water.

And mark this also further: what remains in the water and does not come out through the water, enters into the earth and goes through the earth until it finds its exit <note>original: "Exitum"</note> or is consumed therein, from which earthquakes <note>original: "Erdbidmen"</note> occur. Although these are not the only causes of earthquakes, there are others besides. For in such a manner the wind creates earthquakes, just as waves on the water do when something stands upon it—

<column-break/>

—a ship or a galley; they also tremble and cannot stand still; thus it is a water-earthquake. Thus the wind may also move the pores of the earth—the large and the small, those that are like floodgates or similar—moving the earth also, though not as easily as water, which is much more willing and yielding to it. For everything that is born must be consumed and has an end: so it is also with the wind, which has its limit <term>Terminum: a fixed boundary or end point of existence.</term> which it may not overstep, but must die at that very point and meet its death.

Therefore know that the earth of itself does not produce winds <note>original: "Ventos"</note>, except for what the winds drive into it. Although Vulcan <note>The alchemical spirit or power of fire</note> is also in the earth and in the water, he is not lightning-like <note>original: "fulgurisch"</note> in them; only in the firmament is he lightning-like and possesses a tempestuous impetus <note>original: "Tempestuosischen Impetum"; a stormy, violent force</note>.

This is the nature of the wind, which has the power to pass through all stones and metals, the earth and everything, nothing excluded: and to snap apart and overthrow whatever resists it. By this example you shall finally understand well: like gunpowder <note>original: "Büxenpuluver"</note>, which makes the gun and the ground tremble, and no one can still it.

Thus it is: that just like a gunpowder <note>original: "Büxenpuluer"</note> made of Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury, which nothing can withstand, so too is this force. And although it is not fire, does not smoke, and does not explode, it is nonetheless an ethereal gunpowder that comes from the caverns and emunctories <term>emunctories: outlets or excretory organs; here used to describe the points where celestial forces are "cleansed" or released.</term> of the cataracts of the Vulcanian <note>relating to Vulcan, the Roman god of fire and the forge; in alchemy, this refers to the transformative power of heat.</note> wind-stars. It is, therefore, a windy saltpeter, sulfur, and mercury, which accomplish all things wonderfully. Without these three species <term>three species: the Tria Prima or Three Primaries—Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury—which Paracelsus believed were the fundamental building blocks of all matter.</term>, it is impossible for these things to occur; the only difference is that one is of an earthly nature, while the other is of an ethereal character and property.

### ON THE GENERATION OF
### WATERS. Chapter 6. <note>original: "DE GENERATIONE VNDARVM"</note>

Understand now further that rain has a similar origin. There are certain stars in which the nature of rain exists, and everything belonging to rain is found there in the same way and form as in the wind-stars. These are endowed with the Three Primaries through the First Matter <term>First Matter: original "Prima materia"; the primitive, formless base of all substance.</term> and are provided and ordained for rain, just as a tree is for its fruit. This serves in every way as a perfectly certain and true ex-

<column-break/>

-ample, showing that the visible things among us and the invisible things above us are to be understood in the same way. For it cannot be as is commonly said—that rain is an extraction from the other water, thus ascending and distilling up and down. The reasons for this have been sufficiently shown in the previous sections. Rather, we should know that every rain comes from its own origin and is born anew, having never existed before; just as a spring might arise this year that was never there in the years before. So also are there seeds, where one follows the other and yields its fruit in its time, as God created it.

Now, the number of rain-stars cannot be recorded, for the entire firmament <note>the heavens or the sky</note> is full of them, distributed to all lands across the globe <note>original: "Globul"</note>. And although it happens that in certain regions the effect <note>original: "effectus"</note> of the rain-stars does not always manifest, or it rains seldom or not at all, this is not because the stars are missing. Rather, they are dried out by the winds and constellations <note>original: "constellationen"</note> of that same land and region. Just as a tree or a fruit may be dried out and unable to ripen because of drought, so it also happens with the rain-stars in those

to those places and ends where they then stand.
Likewise, if they stand in moist constellations <term>constellationibus: arrangements of stars believed to influence earthly events.</term> and wind-stars, they give all the more rain; that is, they add to it well, and nothing of them is lost. Likewise, know that if they come late or early, or in great or small amounts, it is not because they have dried out, but because in some years they are more fruitful than in others. Just as we see in all growing things on earth, the same occurs in the firmament <note>The "firmament" refers to the sphere of the sky or heavens.</note>.

Now, know the beginning of rain in this way. When its ethereal paroxysm <term>paroxismus: a sudden outburst or the peak of a natural cycle.</term> arrives—meaning that Autumn <note>original: "Autumnus"</note> is here and ready—then Vulcan <note>The alchemical personification of transformative fire.</note>, the Iliaster <term>Iliaster: in Paracelsian philosophy, the primeval force that gives shape to matter.</term>, and the Archeus <term>Archeus: the "inner chemist" or vital force that governs the functions of nature.</term> begin to prepare within those same stars, as if in a cooking pot, and they work toward rain, dispensing and preparing it. Once they have waited for the time of digestion <term>digestion: an alchemical term for the slow heating of a substance to bring it to maturity.</term>, then the flowers <note>original: "Flores"; here meaning the first visible manifestations or "blooms" of a process.</note> come forth, just as on a tree. First comes a cloud-mass <note>original: "Gewülcke"</note>, which increases according to the capacity of those stars—whether many or large, slow, fast, or nimble, as it may happen and according to the matter present. For first, the ethereal smoke goes forth; and because it is distinct from common earthly smoke, it is called a cloud. Now, this same cloud,

<column-break/>

cloud, when it comes out from the stars, settles downward toward the earth into the air, where it floats. Now, in itself, it is water, but not yet fully prepared. And its matter is Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury <note>original: "Sal, Sul, Merc"; the Tria Prima or three primary building blocks of all matter according to Paracelsus.</note>. All three of these, once outside the stars, undergo resolution <note>original: "Resoluierung"; the process of a solid or vapor turning into liquid.</note>, just as tartar <note>original: "Weinstein"</note> dissolves in a cellar or in water. Thus, the air is the cellar of this calcination <term>calcination: the reduction of a substance to a powder or its essential state through heat.</term> or digestion, in which they dissolve and release themselves.

When the cloud stands in the air as if in a cellar, if it is well-prepared for the solution <note>original: "Solution"</note>, it dissolves all the sooner; if not, it remains longer in the air. Sometimes, if there is very little of it, it vanishes entirely or produces a rain of such slight moisture that it cannot even reach the earth. But if it remains and is ordained for rain, then it resolves <note>original: "resoluiert"</note> itself into water. And according to the nature of the rain, so is the resolution; the rain comes from the clouds, and the clouds come from the stars. Thus it often happens that such a rain lasts continuously, because the cloud-mass daily goes out from the rain-stars and does not cease to increase—either one star following another, or many together, as the harmony <note>original: "Concordantz"</note> dictates. For some give rain for an hour, some more; some for a day, some for many days; some for a week, and some for many weeks.

...weeks, some quite unstable and mixed together, at irregular hours, etc. But however that may be: first, the preparation <term>preparation: the alchemical process of readying a substance for transformation.</term> occurs in the stars from the Three Primes <note>The "Three Primes" (Tria Prima) are Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury, the fundamental components of all matter in Paracelsian thought.</note> according to such a nature. Afterward, the cloud-mass <note>original: "gewülcke"</note> forms. After that, the dissolving <term>resoluierung: the process of a solid or gas turning into liquid; a "resolution."</term> happens in the air, by means of the air. From that same process, the rain falls upon the earth.

Now, the dissolving happens like this: as soon as the rain-cloud enters the air, as if into its cellar <note>Like a cool cellar used for chemical stabilization, the air acts as the vessel where the vapor condenses.</note>, and its time of resolution has arrived, it then dissolves drop by drop from one another. It does not fall in a single mass, but just as if it were being distilled <term>distillation: a process of purifying a liquid by heating and cooling it to collect the drops.</term> downwards. This is prepared alchemically by Divine Providence <note>original: "Diuina prouidentia"</note> so that one drop filters, distills, and drips <note>original: "guttier"</note> after another in the manner of drops, just as they then come down to us. Thus it often happens that the dissolution occurs slowly, which is why the drops fall slowly. At other times it is faster or more nimble, depending on the arrangement and dispensation—whether there are many or few, large or small drops—according to the year and the season.

<margin>From this <unclear>as</unclear> they are here <unclear>Young</unclear></margin>
Thus, every rain is nothing other than a dissolved Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury <note>original: "Sal, Sul. vnd Merc."</note> separated into the form of rain during the first extraction and separation. In the six days when God created all things, He also ordained all things in their place with their own characteristics. These same things further prepare themselves for rain in the fire of Vulcan <note>original: "Igneo Vulcano"; Vulcan is the alchemical personification of transformative fire or the "inner smith" of nature.</note>, just as tartar <term>Tartarus: in this context, a name for stony precipitates or sediments that can be transformed into liquid.</term> prepares itself into water, and other things as well, which in their final material state never look like they did in their first matter <note>original: "Prima materia"; the raw, undifferentiated base substance of a thing.</note>.

<margin>A fine <unclear>wind</unclear>, that is, Altanus <note>Altanus was a classical name for a gentle breeze blowing from the land.</note></margin>
Furthermore, know that such rains of which I speak are the mildest and best rains, which best cool, moisten, and soak the earth, and are also the healthiest. For they spring from the mildest of the Three Primes of the First Matter. For there are others that are more harsh, and therefore they are of a harsher nature than these. These are also the rains that commonly spread far and wide when they reach a concordance <term>concordance: a state where different celestial influences or "stars" align to act as one.</term>—that is, when many of them come together and are prepared at once. Wherever such a concordance occurs, many kinds of rain happen there all mixed together, which can be recognized by the drops: some are very subtle like alcohol <term>Alcool: in early chemistry, this referred to an extremely fine, purified powder or a highly refined essence.</term>, some a little larger, and so on up to the largest, as common experience itself proves.

But now there are other stars from which rain also proceeds, but these have the property of bringing winds with them.

### The Book

This is the reason why the Salt and the Mercury in those same rain-stars are also not quite well-prepared: that is, their wildness <note>original: "impetus"; referring to the raw, unrefined energy of the elements.</note> has not yet been taken from them by Vulcan <note>In alchemy, Vulcan represents the transformative power of fire or heat that refines substances.</note>. And this is a particular nature. Now, where such a nature exists, winds without rain will arise from those same stars for several hours or days, etc. Afterward, when the winds subside, it is a sign that Mercury and Salt have united themselves with the Sulfur; only then do they enter into the cloud-mass <note>original: "Gewülcke"</note> and send it out into the air—sometimes with the wind, or with clouds and wind together. And such rain-clouds, accompanied by their own winds, seldom remain in one place, because the wind is resolved <term>resolution: the process of a vapor turning into a liquid; condensation.</term> at the same place where they fell; instead, they are driven into foreign nations.

So, when these same clouds are resolved in the "cellar" of the etheric resolution, the rain falls down in great quantities, as was mentioned before. It should also be noted that when the wind is within the rain—that is, during the resolution—the wind destroys the resolution and breaks its resolved drops <note>original: "guttas"</note> into two or three parts. It also drives them to fall more quickly than they otherwise would, tossing the drops here and there so that they cannot distill <term>distillation: the alchemical process of a liquid falling in pure drops.</term> according to their nature, as is otherwise proper.

also other rain-clouds—stand upon the mountains, for that has more power to further the process than on flat lands. The reason is that the mountains possess a coolness; this same coolness comes from the mountain range, and when these clouds enter this coolness, they resolve themselves more quickly than if they were consumed in other flat lands. For there are many rain-clouds that are consumed by other constellations <note>original: "Constellationem"; here referring to the overarching astrological/atmospheric conditions.</note>, which cannot happen in the same way in the mountains. Thus it also often happens that the earth and the water, within themselves—through their embodied springs and water and nature—give off a coolness from their own properties. This happens so that the air also becomes cooler, as frequently occurs. ## ON CONGEALMENT
### Chapter VII.

Thus you should know about the congealed things—that is, about freezing, snow, and whatever is of the nature of snow—and the things that make the winter. And first, note in general that these too are only trees—that is, only stars—upon which

So know first of the beginning of such generation <note>The process of coming into being or birth.</note> that there are certain stars which are contrary to the Sun; and because they are contrary to the Sun, when the Sun moves from its exaltation <term>Exaltation: In astrology, the position where a planet or celestial body has its greatest influence; here referring to the Sun's strength in summer.</term> as in winter, these winter stars abound. There is one species of them whose nature is this: everything that the Sun dissolves <note>original: "resoluiert"; to turn a solid into a liquid.</note>, they coagulate <note>original: "coagulieren"; to turn a liquid into a solid.</note>; that is, they turn all water into ice. Now their power is such: you see how God separated from one another the Brightness and Darkness—that is, day and night. You also see how God separated Heat into one place—that is, the Sun—and then Cold into another place—that is, only the winter circle. Thus Heat has the power to dissolve, and Cold has the power to coagulate. When Heat and Cold were together, it was neither hot nor cold; but Divine Alchemy <note>original: "Alchimisterey"</note> separated them, and so the distinct properties came forth.

And just as the heat is in the sun and its stars, so the cold is also enclosed in the stars. And there a free path is given to the heat for its reign according to the season, and to the cold its rule also according to the season, and a limit <term>Terminum: A set boundary or fixed duration of time.</term> is set for both: From which it then follows that each is powerful in its own strength to fulfill its office <term>Officium: A specific duty or natural function ordained by God.</term>. And although sometimes the cold may not appear, for reasons such as being too closely pressed by the warmth—perhaps according to the specific land or the constellation <note>This refers to how geographical location and the alignment of stars can suppress the natural cold of winter.</note>—nevertheless, its power is not taken away. In the same way, the sun may not melt all snow. Therefore it is necessary to note that a resistance also happens to the cold in turn.

So also know from this a further cause of the cold: Fire is a sufficient example through which the sun’s heat may be recognized, as well as what its effect is. But for the cold, there is no public example like fire, except alone in the element of water, in crystals, and other stones. It works in this way: crystals grow out of the water, and the matter <note>original: "Materia"</note> is such that they have within themselves the spirit of coagulation <term>Spiritus coagulationis: The alchemical force or essence that causes liquid to become solid or "clumped" together.</term>; therefore they are coagulated. This happens just as the water is affected by the frigid <note>original: "Frigorischen"; from the Latin *frigor*, meaning cold or cooling.</note> and glaucous <note>original: "Glaueischen"; likely referring to the icy-blue or gray-blue stars associated with the deep cold of winter.</note> stars. In that same place, the winters become the coldest and the summers become the hottest. I shall let this pass for now and leave it to the study of Astronomy.

Now there is another kind of winter star which produces hailstones <note>original: "Grandines"</note> and gems <note>original: "Gemmas"; in this context, Paracelsus refers to a specific, hard, crystalline type of hail that resembles precious stones.</note>. These are not snow, but rather coagulated water; yet they were never liquid water in their primary matter <term>Prima Materia: The fundamental, formless substance from which all physical things are created.</term>, being born as they are. These stars have a special circle to travel, and they are stars of such a nature that they provide their hailstones and gems in all four seasons of the year, regardless of the local climate, the nature of the land, the constellation, the moon, or the wind-stars. They do not arrive at the time of winter snow, nor during any of the rains mentioned in the previous chapter; rather, they arrive only in summer and winter times when they possess cold and wind from within themselves, and when no other contrary force <note>meaning a balancing or opposing element like warmth</note> stands against them. For their generation cannot occur in mild, soft, or gentle weather, but only in harsh weather and harsh times, whether in summer or winter.

Although gems and hailstones differ in how they fall—namely, that hailstones fall most often in April and March, during the thawing of the snow, or at times when the winter stars do not stand mildly—at other times of the year they do not require a harsh season, but may arise during mild weather. But the gems do not come except with turbulence, and they ruin the weather which would otherwise be good, overwhelming <note>original: "vberbachens"; literally "over-baking," but used here to mean stifling or suppressing the mildness of the air.</note> everything that is mild.

And although gems are considered winter stars less often than hailstones—because they appear more in summer than in winter—their generation is nonetheless of a wintery nature, which is why they are reckoned as being of an icy sort <note>original: "Glaueische arth"; likely derived from the Latin *glacies*, meaning ice.</note>. Therefore, what follows is their birth and the primary matter from which they are made, and in what way they are thus ordered, and what purpose they serve; for their appearance is entirely contrary to the natural desires of the elements.

<margin>Gems. Hail.</margin>Now, the nature of these gems is as follows: just as previously described, they are an embodied nature and property arising from Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury <note>The "Three Primes" or *Tria Prima* of alchemy, believed to be the base components of all material things.</note>, which are then separated in their kind from all heat. And there, only cold is present, just as only blackness exists in a piece of coal; all other colors are gone. They exist only in such a state during their ripening <note>original: "zeitigung"</note>, just as has been shown regarding the essence and nature of other stars.

Take also an example from this: If a cold **Sulphur** that is white, a cold **saltpeter** that is fatty, and a **Mercury** that is mild are taken together into a mixture, and these three should merge into one body so that there is nothing else but **Unity** <note>original Latin: "Vnitas"</note>, they are in the beginning so contrary that it takes a long time before they enter into one another, with rushing, boiling, thundering, and similar turbulence. It is the same in the heavens within such stars, for those stars are of an even higher and different nature than the earthly ones; therefore, their essence and manifestation are also more turbulent. Now, as they move toward perfection, two natures separate from each other: those that **coagulate** into one, from which **gems** <note>original Latin: "Gemmæ"; Paracelsus uses this term for a specific, extremely hard, crystal-like form of hail.</note> are made; and the other into the **resolved matter** <note>original Latin: "resolutum"; referring to the liquid state of a dissolved substance</note>, from which

comes a rain. And with both, there is a common wind, cold, and turbulence.
Thus the star casts its matter out into the "cellar" of the air. In that same place, one part **resolves** <note>dissolves into liquid</note> and falls as rain upon the earth; the other part **coagulates** <note>hardens into a solid</note>, for it contains **coagulated Vulcan** <note>latent fire or heat that, paradoxically, drives the hardening and "ripening" process of minerals and ice</note> within it. This creates that body which does not enter into resolution, forming balls—large and even larger, according to the size of the matter—from which **hail** now results.
Likewise, know also of the "little pebbles," that is, of **hailstones** <note>original Latin: "Grandinibus"</note>; they are also formed in this way. Sometimes they come with rain, sometimes not. If it is with rain, it proceeds exactly as stated above; but if it turns into hailstones, it is similar to the aforementioned **gems**, only that such a great **impetus** is not present. For that same cold **Sulphur**, **Mercury**, and **Salt** are not so coarse nor as highly graded in their **impetus**, even though they produce wind and cold; yet it is not of the same nature as the **gems**.
Now, there are always two kinds within such stars: rain and "little stones," as mentioned. And although it is the case that they also separate in this: That which is coagulated <term>Coagulated: Thickened or solidified from a liquid state.</term> has a summery impetus <note>force or violent momentum</note>, for summer storms are wintry; what is in winter is mild and sweet like snow. Therefore, they have a spirit and a primary matter <term>Materia Prima: The fundamental, unformed substance from which all things are created.</term> of a summery nature, and one of a wintry nature, working through their Vulcan <note>In Paracelsian philosophy, "Vulcan" refers to the "inner smith" or the natural force of fire that matures and completes all things.</note>. Their affairs are like hermaphrodites <note>original Latin: "Hermophroditę"</note>, striking into both natures: now they may be summery, then wintry. And there two contraries stand by each other in one body, which cannot be otherwise in any way except in hermaphrodites. For every hermaphrodite that is perfectly produced is suited to both sides. So also are the upper generations—hail and showers—likewise born from both types of weather, and thus both natures are in them. Also like a mule, which is neither donkey nor horse, but its own thing. For that which is cold should have no [force] in it, nor any impetus; but that which stands in the heat has it. Thus, two contraries and workers are harmoniously joined together.

The birth of snow is solely an ordained creation of God, who thus wishes to be seen in His works in various and manifold ways. And there is nothing more to write of the same, except that God also created such trees—which are the stars—in the firmament, so that they give snow in their seasons. Now they are made from the primary matter of the Three Primes <term>The Three Primes: In Paracelsian alchemy, these are Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt—the basic components of all physical matter.</term>, from the cold Three Primes, directed toward the wintry nature, in such a way that they are also prepared within themselves in their decoction <term>Decoction: The alchemical process of heating or ripening a substance.</term> by the ethereal Vulcan, just as the time must come in the earthly Vulcan to give its fruits toward autumn. For all things are set distinctly into an order.

Now the operation is such that the Three Primes cast up their snow-clouds in the form of rain; but when they arrive, the coldness of the air cannot corrupt <note>Here, "corrupt" means to break down or change the state of the substance.</note> them, but it is too cold and dry, leaving them to remain in their essence. Therefore, as soon as they emerge from before the star, they granulate and drop into sparks—that is, into snow-sparks—and fly to the earth, for they have a mild and gentle nature within them. Thus it also often happens that rain and snow fall together; yet not from rain-stars, but because the snow is so mild and subtle that some part of it dissolves <note>original German: "resoluiert"</note>

<summary>Paracelsus explains that snow and hail are "hermaphroditic" weather phenomena that combine both summer (heat/force) and winter (cold/mildness) qualities. He describes stars as "celestial trees" that produce snow through a ripening process governed by an ethereal fire.</summary>

# Book IV /

Description: why and how snow [exists], or why and how hail [exists], is not necessary to know, as it is written: So has He <note>Referring to the Creator.</note> created it, so that what has become of it is not for man to know; only what man makes himself, that he may know how and when.

### CONCERNING LIGHTNING <note>original Latin: "DE FVLGVRE"</note>
### Chapter VIII.

Now take further note of this action of the Firmament: namely concerning the lightning-bolt <note>original: "Stral," literally "ray" or "beam," here meaning a thunderbolt</note>, which is the greatest and strongest work of the Firmament among all other meteorological actions. For if the Firmament did not let itself be noticed visibly and substantially in this point, it would not be possible to believe that such a power could be presumed to exist in the nature of the Firmament: so wondrously beyond all understanding, yet not beyond Nature, but within Nature: yet a great thing for man to contemplate. From this, it is proven evidently that God has shaped Sulphur, Salt, and Mercury <note>original: "Sul, Sal, Merc." These are the *Tria Prima* or Three Primes, the foundational elements of Paracelsian chemistry.</note> into the Firmament, from which all things are born materially.

Now, although this type is also among the meteorological actions, it is nonetheless distinct from all others. For some stars are in the first degree, others in the second, the third, etc., up to 32 degrees: In these 32 degrees stand the **lightning-stars** <term>Stralsternen: Celestial bodies or forces responsible for producing thunderbolts and lightning.</term> so highly graded in their Three Primes <note>original: "Primis tribus"</note> of the First Matter <note>original: "Primæ materiæ"</note>.

In this, it is also to be noted that they also have among them those that become errant and falter in their operation, or are too early, such that they sometimes even come in winter: however, when they come, such an impetus is there that they ask nothing of time, the moon, wind-stars, etc.; it breaks through. And the signs of their arrival are great heat, which are the precursors; which heat comes alone from their **decoction** <term>Decoction: In alchemy, the process of heating a substance to refine it or bring it to maturity.</term> and preparation, fast or slow, as it is prepared and found in the First Matter through Vulcan <term>Vulcanum: Named for the Roman god of fire, this refers to the alchemical force of heat that transforms and "cooks" matter into its final form.</term>.

The spirit and Mercury <note>original: "Merc.," representing the volatile or transformative principle in alchemy.</note> are contrary in them both—that is, sharper than nitre <note>original: "Nitrum," or saltpeter.</note> and more powerful than sulfur <note>original: "Sulphur."</note>. From these three are born thunder, lightning-bolts <note>original: "stral," literally "ray" or "beam," used here for a thunderbolt.</note>, and storms, which are the greatest works of the whole firmament.

When the time for this generation <term>generation|the process of a substance being born or coming into being</term> matures <note>original: "Autumniert," literally "to become like autumn," meaning to ripen or reach a peak.</note> and wishes to become ready, the compound <note>original: "compositum"</note> of Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury (as has been said) is in all its highest strength and power. When they are thus joined together, Vulcan <note>In alchemy, Vulcan represents the internal fire or the "artisan" of nature that transforms raw matter into finished products.</note> begins to prepare them into the Ultimate Matter <note>original Latin: "Vltima materia," the final form of a substance.</note>, which is the lightning-bolt. In this beginning, the work enters its increase <note>original: "augment"</note>, and the degrees of fire <margin>* rise</margin> also increase one after another to the highest point. The increase of these fiery degrees increases the heat of the summer, so that the summer becomes hotter than it should naturally be of itself; it climbs to the highest peak until the preparation by Vulcan is completed.

Sometimes it happens that the Prime Matter <note>original Latin: "Materia prima," the base state of a substance before it is refined.</note> does not reach its end and thus dissipates; afterwards, the remaining heat also subsides. Sometimes a small storm comes of it—that is, like common rain—but then it subsides. Sometimes it is delayed, so that over a long time only a little bit comes of it at a time, and it does not come together into one strike <note>original: "klippen," suggesting a sharp crack or collision.</note>. Sometimes, out of the Three <meta>catchword: Primes (Tribus)</meta>

<column-break/>

Primes <note>original Latin: "Tribus primis," referring to the Three Primes of Paracelsian theory.</note>, one is not perfect, and so nothing comes of it. Sometimes it enters a cloud and does the same, failing because the nitre and mercury are not sufficient in their "tyranny" <note>original: "Tyrannitet," a term used to describe the extreme potency or violent internal force of a substance.</note>; thus, nothing comes of it.

But where such hindrances are absent and the process goes forward, there Nature displays her species and the materials of the firmament, showing what subjects she has and what she has mastered. Thus occurs the composition of the lightning-bolt's birth: the highest degrees of the three, as mentioned, each in its most tyrannical and powerful state. Thus, the saltpeter <note>original: "Salniter."</note> is most powerful when it lives in its "fatness" <note>original: "feiste," referring to its oily, combustible potency.</note>, for its fatness is its tyranny. Likewise, Mercury is in its "contrariety," so that it is against all things and more "evil" <note>In this context, "evil" refers to being reactive, volatile, or corrosive.</note> in all ways than any other adversary. And the sulfur is the toughest, most subtle body <note>original Latin: "Corpus."</note>, mixed with the others into a perfect substance.

And the thunder is a snap from the opposition of the sulfur, mercury, and saltpeter <term>Salnitri</term>, which cannot stand to be together.

### The Book / METEOROLOGY

<sig>O</sig>

When the completion <term>Perfection</term> begins—in such a way that the mass <term>Massa|The alchemical mixture of Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury</term> in that place turns into a beam, uniting and perfecting itself within its own substance—know that this mass is nothing other than a well-sealed container <note>original: "Büxe", literally a box or jar, often used to describe a pressure vessel or even a cannon</note>. The closer it gets to "birth," the firmer its skin becomes, and at last, when it is fully ripe, it lies within its shell just like an egg in its shell. Now that it is prepared and lies in such a shell, it follows that the other clouds <note>original: "Gewülcke"</note> nearby are born only for rain; they dissolve <term>resolution|The transformation of a substance from a solid or gas into a liquid state</term> into water. 

And when this dissolving begins, wind and force <term>impetus</term> move through the mass, for its clouds are all born for the storm <term>tempestat</term>, intended to be completed with turbulence. When the mass lying in the shell feels the moisture from the clouds—that is, when the wetness reaches the mass—it is exactly like someone pouring water into molten bell-metal <note>original: "glocken speise"; the bronze alloy used for casting bells reacts violently and can explode if it comes into contact with water while molten</note> or the like. As soon as the water touches the mass, it bursts apart into all parts <term>Partes</term> of the world. In that same crack, there is a heavenly flash, swift and nimble. From that moment, as many pieces as break, so many thunders occur; every crack is a thunder. In that same thunder comes the true crack, so that a thunder precedes it from the true

<column-break/>

true crack, and because it strikes, the crack moves with it in the thunder.

When this has happened, that same mass strikes fire out toward the heights—that is, toward the Earth—as if at a target. Just as it first sensed the open hole, it moves forward, not backward; for it breaks open forward, not backward. Regarding this, know that when it comes out, its nature is recognized: namely, a body <term>Corpus</term> that is nothing but a fire brought into a small mass, larger or smaller according to its kind. This body is compacted together, and yet is spiritual <term>Spiritualisch</term>, airy <term>Aerisch</term>, and etheric <term>Etherisch</term>. From this nature comes a power of penetration <term>penetration</term> that no one can deny; it passes through everything like a spirit that has neither body, blood, nor flesh. No one can adequately describe or speak of its passage. 

Furthermore, it moves with such a swift stroke and such force <term>impetu</term> that everything in its way must fall to the ground and yield to it. This fire is of such a high and enduring heat <note>original: "perhennisch", likely meaning intense, perpetual, or "ever-burning" like a celestial fire</note> that in an instant it melts all the iron it touches. break and melt, and do nothing to the leather: That they often strike a man dead, and no one sees anything: That they often melt the lead on windows, and do nothing to the glass: That they often burn the skull, and do nothing to the brain: That they strike a horse dead through a saddle, and nothing happens to the saddle: That they burn and singe away bed-covers <note>original: "Betzichen", the linen cases used for featherbeds</note> entirely, and nothing happens to the feathers: That they often burn a coat, and nothing happens to the body: That they often melt money in a locked chest, and nothing happens to the wood. It is possible for no one to sufficiently describe the wondrous nature and effect of the bolt. 
   For my part, I cannot consider such a cause to be natural: either such wondrous working must come from it being an example <term>exemplum</term> to us—that Christ will also come so wondrously that he will not harm the bruised reed, nor quench the spark on the wick <note>A reference to Isaiah 42:3/Matthew 12:20; the author suggests Christ's power, like lightning, is selective and supernatural</note>, but will indeed destroy the other things that surround it. Or the celestial powers <term>Superi|Those dwelling in the higher spheres</term> must employ such art: Or the devil must do such things. Nevertheless, I abide by the first [reason], since Christ has placed himself as the example, to come down in this way...

# The Book of / METEORS <note>original: METEORORVM</note>

O iij

...come, and to judge the living and the dead; so that he, particularly beyond all human reason, performs special signs here, so wonderful that on the Last Day his will shall also come to pass as quickly, agilely, and wonderfully.

Now, however, at the beginning of this meteorological description, I mention the *Pennates* <note>winged spirits or household gods in Roman mythology, here used to describe celestial agents</note>, that is, the *Superos* <note>original: "Superos," Latin for those who dwell above or celestial beings</note> in the firmament: these same ones are those who are masters in such work, to prepare and be agile, and are those who lead and point the bolt; that is, they are Master Gunners <term>Büchsenmeister|The technical officers in charge of artillery; the author envisions them as spiritual engineers of the weather</term> and aim the piece of ordnance <note>referring to the lightning bolt as a weapon of heaven</note>, namely to where Christ wants, specifically at that which is not in the way of God—that is, into the enemies of God and not into His friends. For this is proven in the example that Christ will do no one injustice, only say: "Depart, you cursed ones, into the eternal fire."

Therefore, such things are presages <note>original: "præfagia," meaning omens or forewarnings</note>, not only the bolt, but every storm that moves so violently, with tyrannical force <note>original: "impetu"</note>, thunder, wind, hail, and showers; everything is included here. Therefore, let those whom it strikes look up; Sodom and Gomorrah were also struck, etc. These are grave presages; they serve as an example of how Christ will come.

Likewise, it is no less true that while the Devil possesses man and makes him so violent, as is then publicly known, and has power also to possess the livestock, such as the swine: if this is permitted to him, then it is also permitted to him to possess dogs, cats, horses, and everything that man has; and it is also permitted to him to possess the weather and bolt, showers, and hail, and to create such violence, and diligently to shoot wherever he pleases, provided God allows it.

However things may be, it happens by the permission of God. Therein it is well to remember: He will also send the Devil to the earth in this way, so that he may take his own followers with a violence into the eternal fire. In the wisdom and will of God, nothing is to be attributed to a man, but only to be contemplated; for which I am prompted by the fact that He <note>Christ</note> has prefigured Himself there, and will not break a bruised reed, nor extinguish a smoldering wick <note>A reference to Isaiah 42:3 and Matthew 12:20, signifying Christ's mercy toward the weak</note>: but indeed He will break the other, which is whole and burning high. This is then well understood in the thundering and storms.

Thus the origin of the bolt and the clouds (like) storms in the firmament, against the earth, is a wonderful growth, and even much more wonderful in its works.

The author uses the metaphor of the "bruised reed" to contrast God's mercy for the humble with the violent judgment awaiting the wicked during the Second Coming.</summary>

### OF EXTRANEOUS THINGS
### Chapter IX.

Furthermore, one must also know about the generation <note>meaning the birth or spontaneous production</note> of those things such as frogs, worms, or other living animals that are occasionally born from above and found below. Regarding this, know that I mentioned at the beginning of the "Animals in Heaven" <note>likely a reference to a previous section or book</note> how no element exists that does not have a living creature within it—that is, of many kinds. Just as there are many kinds of worms in the earth, and many kinds in the water, and likewise in the air, so it is also in the firmament <term>Firmament|In this context, the "upper air" or the celestial sphere where weather is generated, believed to be a distinct environment with its own life</term>. For no one should wonder at this; Nature is a mother within the elements, regarding both the upper and the lower ones.

<margin>Frogs.</margin>
From this it follows that when it is said to have rained frogs, such frogs are from the firmament; and in the firmament, their kind is born from those like them. For certain reasons, they have come down to the earth. Just as the example demonstrates how moles <note>original: "Schermeuße," referring to burrowing rodents like moles or voles</note> often come out of the earth and run about, even though that is not their usual way; the nature of certain worms is also

<column-break/>

the same. Thus, it is no less true that such creatures are also found in heaven and come out of their own region.

Although it is not possible for something to fall down upon us from above—because it is "upwards" from us, just as if we were to "fall up" into heaven—it is nonetheless true that they fall down. However, for them, the direction is "up," as is the nature of heaven <note>The author suggests a relativistic view of "up" and "down" between the earthly and celestial spheres</note>. Yet this cannot happen unless a storm <note>original: "Wetter"</note> causes a destruction in those places, and they fall down in that same impetus <note>violent force</note>, so that they enter into the rain and similar conditions. It is also the case that "seed" <note>original: "Sperma," referring to generative biological matter or "life-germs"</note> falls down during the month of May, from which tree frogs are formed. For all tree frogs are from the firmament and fall down in the manner of seed.

<margin>Tree frogs.</margin>
Therefore, an animal is often found in a place where its parents never went. This is the fault of the firmament in some cases; in others, the air is the cause, which also contains such generating principles <note>original: "generantia"</note> in many forms within itself, just as much as the earth or the water. Such is the birth of the Basilisks <note>mythical serpents born from eggs without a mother of their own species</note> and many new, unheard-of animals whose origins are without earthly mothers, contrary to all other animals of the earth and water.

<summary>This page discusses the phenomenon of animals like frogs and worms seemingly falling from the sky, attributing their origin to the "firmament" which, as an element, possesses its own life forms. The author explains that these creatures are displaced by violent storms and can also be generated from celestial "seed" falling to earth, as seen with tree frogs and Basilisks.</summary>

It is no less true / it often happens / that people say / it is **raining blood**: This is how it occurs. Now, if that same foam enters into a **mucilaginous** or **bituminous** <term>Bitumen|A thick, oily, or tar-like mineral substance</term> nature / then first / and second it becomes red: For its color is red / and the substance is similar in thinness and thickness to **blood** / a little less or more. Thus it also distills itself down / sometimes by itself / sometimes with a rain / as it then happens. The **frogs** in the firmament are not a substance <note>meaning they do not have a permanent material form there</note> / but they *become* substance / then they also fall down.

The Heavenly Winged Spirits <note>original: "Superi Pennates"; in Paracelsian thought, these are spiritual entities inhabiting the upper air</note> are the carriers <note>original: "Portores"</note> in such matters and create these figures. They cast them down to the Earth, whether upon people, wood, or stone; in short, wherever people might see them. For they are as certain of their aim as the Sun is certain to strike the Earth with its brilliance.

But now take note of the reason: this does not happen unless an omen <note>original: "Præsagium"</note> is at hand. As in my own time, crosses, roosters, scourges, [the figure of] Pilate, etc., have fallen upon the people, in such a form as has been written. But this is not from Nature, but from the ordinance of God as a sign <note>original: "Figur"</note> of a new transformation <note>original: "alteration"</note> within them, which precedes and accompanies such signs, as has been proven, and still is.

Thus, such figures often occur in the Heavens, which mean nothing other than such a future event, as they are sometimes used. For instance, in recent years, a King was seen in Silesia <note>original: "Schlesij"</note> with many strange appearances and persons: it is an omen <note>original: "Præsagium"</note> that such a thing will also be seen and fulfilled in its own time. For the Heavenly Winged Spirits <note>original: "Pennates superi"</note> are the masters, workers, preparers, arrangers, and shapers of such omens.

Therefore, everyone should know that such things are not otherwise than from Nature, made by...

...made by the Heavenly Winged Spirits <note>original: "Pennates superi"</note>, commanded from the inner Heaven to announce the future of the time. <margin>Stone and Metals.</margin>

Just as the Earth has plants <note>original: "Plantæ"</note>, the water has minerals <note>original: "Mineral"</note>, and the air has manna <term>Tereniabin: A Paracelsian term for a celestial substance or "dew of heaven" that falls from the air</term>, so also the Heaven has its own generations. Therefore, it is not possible for water or Earth to generate the lightning bolt, rain, or snow; the Heaven alone must do it, as it is its duty <note>original: "officium"</note>. ...belongs to metals. Therefore, no <term>mineral</term> <note>meaning a substance typically formed within the earth</note> can be born from heaven; thus, neither stone nor <term>iron</term> <note>original: "Eyfen"</note>.

But now know that it has been proven that such things have happened: true, natural stones have fallen from heaven. It is also to be believed that metals have likewise fallen, yet none other than <term>iron</term>. For there is a specific cause in both [stone and iron] that prevents other things from falling down in this way, except <term>iron</term> and a specific kind of stone. 

For it happens thus: You see that all stars have an attractive power within them. The <term>Sun</term> draws to itself what is moist. The <term>Moon</term> draws to itself what is warm in nature; thus, the Sun draws the cold to itself—that is, the water, which is visible. Conversely, the Moon [draws] what is warm—that is, wherever a warm nature exists in a creature, it draws it out. From this it follows that people from whom it draws warmth, when they are burdened with illnesses, feel it: if the illness comes from cold, and there is no illness (or only a small one) in the warmth. But as soon as the Moon enters its <term>magnetic nature</term> <note>its attractive phase or property</note>, it withdraws the warmth from the person; therefore, they become sicker, which otherwise would not happen. Thus it also draws the life out of many who are under its influence quite <meta>catchword: little/</meta>

<column-break/>

little. And what it does not do, because it is not suited for its nature, other stars do in other ways.

Regarding this, know now that there are also other stars that have a <term>magnetic nature</term>, which draw <term>iron</term> to themselves. Otherwise, no metal can be drawn upward. When such an attraction begins, it is certain that <term>iron</term> will be drawn upward into the <term>mansions</term> <note>original: "Mansiones"; the specific celestial regions or "houses" where stars reside</note> of that same star. Just as the mountain <term>Magnes</term> <note>the legendary magnetic mountain often cited in medieval lore</note> naturally proves on Earth that it draws iron toward itself, this does not happen all at once in a single piece. Instead, it gathers it together portion by portion, as far as it can follow, until it can bring a great deal together. 

So too the star <term>Magnes</term> <note>a specific magnetic star</note> draws <term>iron</term> to itself in small form, from the places and ends nearest to it, into its <term>mansion</term>. Once it has it, the star then <term>scrapes</term> <note>original: "Radiert"; here meaning to refine or erode by friction</note> and melts it like a <term>magnet</term>, letting the rust <note>original: "Eruginem" (Latin *aerugo*)</note> fall back down from it, and yields an <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Alcool"; in Paracelsian alchemy, this refers to a highly purified, subtle powder or essence, not liquid alcohol</note>. <margin>Iron.</margin>
But concerning the <term>iron</term> that falls back down, note its nature. That same <term>Ethereal Magnet</term> <note>original: "Etherische Magnes"</note> draws the iron to itself, as was said; for it is not fixed, but transmuted into a <term>rust</term> <note>original: "Eruginem," from the Latin *aerugo*, meaning copper rust or verdigris</note>, which the Magnet draws to itself after it has prepared it. When such iron comes to it, if there is too much of it and the <term>Vulcan</term> <note>referring to the internal alchemical fire or the "smith" of nature</note> is too fast—or if a <term>Vulcan</term> enters into it and catches it with the <term>Ethereal Borax</term> <note>original: "Etherische Borras," a celestial flux used to melt metals</note>—it may cause it to flow together into a single mass, as if it were in a fire-oven or a smelting furnace. And once it has thus become a physical <term>body</term>, the Firmament can no longer carry it and lets it fall. Wherever such iron lies or is found, it cannot be otherwise: it must be better than other iron, for the Ethereal Borax has refined and graded it. But this is not a common work; it is Nature showing herself in this way.

It may also well happen that such generations, which are drawn up from below, are often seized by the <term>Pennates</term> <note>traditionally Roman household gods, but here referring to spirits or elemental forces inhabiting the air</note> who manufacture with them and then let them fall. For the <term>Superiors</term> <note>original: "Superi," the higher celestial beings or forces</note> dwell in the magnetic stars, and their art is strong in such matters.

is, but a strange thing and not without an <term>omen</term> <note>original: "Presagium," a forewarning or sign</note>; even the metal is not without an omen. The generation of such stones is in the stars: for the ray, which is cold, is nothing other than a hard sand, like any other stone. If then the <term>Olympian</term> <note>pertaining to the heavens or the firmament</note> nature gives birth to a stone from a cold ray, it is also quite possible that such stones fall into the earth without a ray.

And because it is publicly proven that they are omens and are strong <term>signs</term> <note>original: "signa"</note> of future things, the <term>higher spirits</term> <note>original: "Pannates superi," likely referring to the Penates or higher elemental beings in Paracelsian thought</note> are those who manufacture and bring together such <term>matter</term> <note>original: "Materiam"</note>. For they indeed have seeds from the <term>cold influx of generation</term> <note>original: "kalter influxion generationis," referring to the astrological influence that causes things to be born or formed</note>, but not gathered together, nor intended to become stone, but rather to resolve themselves in other ways. But the <term>Higher Ones</term> <note>original: "Superi"</note> are those who, for the sake of the omen, do not allow this same matter to reach resolution, but instead make a stone out of it; which stone, when the hour comes, they let fall into the earth.

For the idea that stones are drawn up from the earth is not according to nature, nor is there anything like it; for they are <term>fixed</term> <note>stable and heavy</note> and remain lying on the earth. But concerning the <term>iron</term>, because that has happened, it is not to be contradicted: for since the magnet draws it to itself, it is easy to consider and find in Nature

<meta>catchword: to</meta>

<header>OF METEOROLOGY.</header>

to be found, that there are also magnets in heaven that do this, as has been said. And everything that exists in a state of <term>humidity</term>, and likewise whatever on earth allows itself to rot and <term>putrefy</term> <note>original: "putrificieren"</note> into a moisture—it is all possible that it be drawn up, like rotten wood and other decaying things, for as long as it takes until nothing more remains.

So know now, regarding several special points that occur in the <term>meteoric</term> fashion, how to understand them as mentioned. For nature is wonderful in her works, and strange, and great, ordered and endowed by God. # The Book / CONCERNING EXHALATIONS <note>original: "DE EXHALATIONIBUS"</note>. Chapter 10.

<margin>Star-shot.</margin>
Take note further, then, that the stars purify themselves—both summer and winter stars—not with heavenly [spirit], but with a *Body* <note>original: "Corpus"</note>; that is, they cast a *Body* from themselves, which is called the <term>star-shot</term> <note>original: "Sterngeschoß," a term for meteorites or "star jelly," a gelatinous substance historically believed to be deposited on Earth during meteor showers</note>. Now the origin is this: they have their nourishment in the *Firmament*, sustaining themselves from that same element. For nothing was created that does not require its daily feeding, just as Man does; from which it follows that they also eat from their element and have their nourishment. And whatever sustains itself with food and drink must also give its <term>excrement</term> <note>original: "Excrementum"</note>. It follows, then, that they are sustained by nothing but fire, just as Man is sustained by the Earth from which he comes. From this same fire they draw out their <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Essentiam"</note>, just as Man draws out his blood and flesh. Whatever is not of the *essence*, that same part will be cast off as an *excrement*. If it happens by day, no one sees it; if it happens by night, it is seen. If it is a fiery star that stretches out long, it comes down from above from the heaven of the stars, though one cannot see from how high it falls,

---

<header>OF METEORS <note>original: "METEORORVM"</note>.</header>
<page-num>63</page-num>

falls, but only when it comes down into the lower heavens in the Air; there it becomes visible and falls to the ground; there it lies, and is just like a <term>jelly</term> <note>original: "Sultz," likely referring to the translucent mucilage known as Nostoc or star jelly</note>.

Now it is no less true that many stars purify themselves in other ways that do not appear so fiery, and yet are fire, though invisible. Therefore, many white star-shots are seen, cast from the cold stars; much also passes away in the rain, much in cloudbursts, much in showers and great hail, for these same weather patterns are the stars' <term>purgation</term> <note>original: "purgaz"</note>, moving toward expulsion. But those that were fire become like one who drinks water, from whom water goes out again: so they drink fire, and it goes from them again. As soon as it touches the Earth, however, it is earthly and no longer fire, but a jelly and a slime, like red-colored or yellow-colored <term>frog-spawn</term> <note>original: "froschleich"</note>.

<margin>Bird Dragons.</margin>
You should also know that Nature also makes birds that are fiery and fly—namely, those called **Dragons** <note>original: "Tracken," referring to fireballs, bolides, or long-lasting meteors</note>—which shoot from one mountain to another. These dragons are sometimes a **substantial fire** that they ignite, like a **lightning bolt**; sometimes they become a jelly like the star-shot; sometimes they consume themselves entirely; sometimes in consuming themselves they produce a foul stench.

# The Book / OF METEORS <note>original: "METEORORVM"</note>.

64

The origin of these things is as follows: You see that living creatures often arise from many things—such as beetles, flies, and spiders coming from the air—which do not have seeds from which they originate, but instead must come into being without seed or <term>sperm</term> <note>original: "Sperma," used here in the sense of reproductive biological material</note>, just as natural philosophy teaches us to recognize concerning the air. In the same way, the **Olympus** <note>here referring to the firmament or celestial realm</note> gives birth in its firmament to a species of animals. These are not only **Dragons** <note>a historical term for certain types of fireballs or meteors</note>, but also other things that have life within them and an animal-like intellect, enabling them to shoot and fly from one place to another, whether it be by day or by night.

For just as the element of Water has many sea-monsters within it, the Earth many strange worms, and the Air many strange beetles and gnats, so too does the Heaven have many strange **Salamanders** within it. For these are the true **Salamanders**, born out of the fire—that is, out of the <term>putrefaction</term> <note>original: "putrificatio"; in alchemy, the process of decay or breakdown that allows for new generation</note> of burning sulfur. This is one of the four wonders within the four elements. They are the cattle and animals of **Olympus**; they are as intelligent as the beasts in the water, earth, and air, for they are endowed with the same animal-like understanding. Their life is not long, but while they live, they have a wide range for shooting and a wide circle for flying or darting—namely, for several hundred miles. This is not done with a single shot,

<column-break/>

shot, but over a period of time until they reach one place, and then further to another. As soon as they land, they do not appear fiery; however, as soon as they return briefly to the air, they become fiery again. And as soon as they become fiery, they shine once more. They also have a period of sleep and rest, during which sleep they do not glow, but only during <term>motion</term> <note>original: "motion," referring to their movement through the sky</note>, etc., when they fly or move across the distance.

Now it often happens that one sees two or three **Suns**, or two or three **Moons**, or other shapes appearing more often than they should naturally exist. When this happens, it is solely because it is an **Omen** <note>original: "Præsagium"</note> and not a natural growth of nature. Rather, the **Higher Winged Beings** <note>original: "Pennates superi," likely referring to celestial spirits or powers inhabiting the upper air</note> create them, whereby they prefigure a likeness of what is to come in the future. As has often occurred, these sights have been "stabbed" <note>likely referring to atmospheric phenomena appearing to be pierced by celestial swords, or being interpreted as such</note>, and subsequently the intellect has sufficiently understood what they signified.

Now, they are made in this way by the **Higher Winged Beings**: just as there might be a tree that exists by nature, and an artist comes along and makes one just like it out of wax. Or, there are corals that exist by nature, and an artist comes and makes them through art so that from a distance one looks like the other, and they would

65

R

<meta>catchword: Where</meta>

...three or four etc. would all be considered good corals, for from the visible authentic ones, the names follow the others as well. Or if there were a golden vessel in a place, where it was known in good faith to be golden, and someone made two or three vessels of brass exactly like it and placed them there as well, all three would rightfully be regarded as gold.

Thus the <term>Pennates</term> <note>original: "Pennates"; in the Paracelsian tradition, these are invisible celestial spirits or powers that create atmospheric phenomena and omens</note> have enough material in the **firmament** that they can make as many such "brass suns" <note>likely referring to parhelia or "mock suns," which appear as replicas of the sun</note> as they wish from such brass, and even more of the like. But they have a fixed number, so they do not make more than is necessary for the **presage** <note>original: "præsagium"; a sign or omen of a future event</note>. For that is why the **Pennates** are in the heavens: to wait upon such service. And he who can grasp such knowledge—what the **Pennates** prefigure in such a form—will see that nothing significant or imminent is born or begun that they do not first prefigure. This I entrust to the **Magi** <note>those who study the hidden or spiritual secrets of nature</note>, and not at all to the **Astrologers** and **Astronomers**.

Thus, not only in the Sun and Moon, but also in other burning stars, they make the star itself larger or create another star like it, a new one, of such strange nature in the heavens; all this is their work.

<margin>Two kinds of rainbows.</margin>
In this way, the **Pennates** also create rings or rainbows around the Sun and Moon, or many other such signs of all kinds of colors. They also create rainbows when a rainbow is meant to be a **presage**. Otherwise, there are other rainbows that are natural. However, because God specifically signified and established a peace through the rainbow, wherever such peace is yet to be, or become, or arise, such a rainbow has appeared; these are made and placed solely by the **Pennates**. Therefore, one should distinguish these rainbows specifically from natural ones.

The natural ones come about thus: they are nothing but **Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury** <note>original: "Sul. Sal, vnd Merc."; the three primary alchemical principles, or "Tria Prima," believed to be the building blocks of all matter</note> along with their colors, being digested at the best and highest degree in that place. They are born in the **firmament** from the **water stars**; when these stars wish to create rainy weather, this is the **blossom** <note>original: "plust" or "blust"; a metaphor for the precursor or flowering stage of a process</note> thereof. And rain never comes without a prior blossom. When a star intends to turn into rain, the rainbow is first its blossom, and thereafter comes the rain.

Now, it is no less true that they do not appear as often as it rains; that is, they are not all visible, but vanish into the air and consume themselves.

# The Book /

Where, however, powerful outbursts of rain occur, there powerful rainbows appear; these descend from the stars and clouds down to the earth, and stand with their two points on the ground, with the **arc** <note>original: "Rotundiert," referring to the rounded shape or curvature</note> above them, and they stand for as long as the air is consumed. And thus it often happens that two appear; these are **Gemini** <note>literally "twins"; here referring to a double rainbow</note>, signifying a twofold long rain, for the one signifies itself.

These are God’s order, thus provided and ordained by Divine **Providence** <note>original: "prouidenz"</note>. For there is nothing that does not yield a **blossom** <note>original: "plüest"; Paracelsus uses the metaphor of a plant blooming to describe the culmination or visible manifestation of natural processes</note>. The **lightning** <note>original: "himlizē"; an archaic term for the flash of a storm</note> is a blossom of the weather and the thunderbolt. Likewise, in many other kinds of fruits, there are many blossoms that we cannot see and of which we have no knowledge, because they are consumed and pass away in the air. We see only that which falls down and arrives, etc.

THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS

## Book of the Womb <note>original: "Liber de Matrice"</note>.

Now, beyond all that I have said concerning the ailments of humanity, all has not yet been told. For when we go to the foundation of <term>Medicine</term> <note>original: "Artzney"</note>, there are still other <term>Theoretical</term>, <term>Physical</term>, and <term>Astronomical</term> matters found within the human being, beyond all that has been presented. Now, these same matters concern the <term>Matrix</term> <note>The womb or uterus; in Paracelsian thought, it is seen as a "microcosm" within the female body with its own internal heaven and earth</note> alone, relating only to women.

Now, since the further foundation in this book is none other than to describe that same <term>Matrix</term> and its ailments, in the same way that a human's <note>referring here to the general or male anatomy previously discussed</note> ailments are described: know then that I speak of invisible things. For who is there who has seen in the <term>Anatomy of the Matrix</term> that which I shall hereafter present? Although nothing visible is at that place, it shall nevertheless be analyzed as if it were visible. In the air we see nothing, yet we feel it; so it is also in the <term>Womb</term> <note>original: "Mutter"</note>. Therefore, in the air we may well observe what we feel but do not see, for observation is not commanded to the eyes alone, but
