The **Small World** <term>Microcosm</term> is, however, Man. He is also enclosed thus with a skin, so that his blood, his flesh, and whatever else constitutes Man, should not be in fellowship with the **Great World** <note>the outer universe</note>; that is, so that his **elements** <note>original: "Element"</note> do not touch the external ones in their **substance** <note>original: "Substantz"</note>, lest one should destroy the other. Therefore, Man has the skin upon him; it *is* the Man, separating the two worlds from each other—the Great and the Small, that is, the World and Man—so that two contrary things do not fall together into one world. Thus the World remains in its housing, whole and unbroken, and no one is in that same house to disturb or break it. Thus Man also remains in his house, that is, in his skin. Now it follows from this that it is not Man alone, nor the World alone, but there is yet another world, and it is the smallest, and it is the **Womb**. This is also one world, and is bound within its own vessel; that is, it has its own special vessel, skin, and tissue, so that it also stands for itself. And it is separated from the Small World. Thus **Man** <note>referring specifically to the male here</note> is the Small World; the **woman** <note>original: "fraw"</note> has within her a **specific condition** <note>original: "gepresten"; this term usually means a physical ailment or infirmity, but here Paracelsus uses it to describe the "added" nature of the womb that differentiates the female from the male microcosm.</note>; she is the smallest world, and is something other than the man, and has her...

Now the World has its philosophy and art, so does the man, and so too the woman. In the World, worms grow; in the man, and so too in the woman. But all this is to be understood thus: that all three are one creature, alike in **astronomy**, **philosophy**, and **theory**. monarchy of the world exists, a particular one of the man, and a particular one of the woman. And although these three monarchies are separated from one another, it is nonetheless one thing within the monarchies, for they do not separate from one another in the **Art** <note>referring to the "Great Art" of medicine and alchemy</note>, but rather the Art encompasses them all together as one.

The beginning of this Art is the World, which encompasses the four elements as they lie in their **mother** <note>here referring to the *materia prima* or the source from which elements arise</note>. The middle is Man, whom the **concordance** <note>the harmonious correspondence between the outer universe and the inner person</note> of both encompasses. The third teaching and the last is Woman. Now the physician is whole and perfect in his theory, reasoning, etc., apart from those things which belong to the [common] physicians. # The Book of the / **WOMB** <note>original: "MATRICIS"</note> 69
**S**
<meta>catchword left: and; catchword right: the</meta>

Flesh is **Salt**, **Mercury**, and **Sulfur**; the earth is also **Mercury**, **Salt**, and **Sulfur**; the sea is also **Mercury**, **Salt**, and **Sulfur**, and so on with the others. The elements divide and part themselves for this reason: they are "Mothers," and therefore they must be divided. Their **bodies** <note>original: "Corpora"</note>, however, remain **Sulfur**, **Salt**, and **Mercury**. <note>Paracelsus believed these three substances, the "Tria Prima," were the fundamental building blocks of all matter.</note> Now, the woman is something else, yet she is also **Mercury**, **Salt**, and **Sulfur**, just as much as the world and just as much as the man. Therefore, know this of her: because she is, in this **substance** <note>original: "Substantz"</note>, such a **body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note> that she is a world herself. But because she is different from the world and the man, her **physics** <note>original: "Physica"</note> is also different. And because she is a world, she is equal to the man and the world in her diseases, yet different and distinct, for she has a different **office** <note>original: "officium"; meaning a specific function or duty in the order of nature.</note>. Each specific office separates her **theoretical physics** <note>original: "Physica Theorica"</note> from the others. Therefore, although women may suffer from and acquire **dropsy**, **jaundice**, **paralysis**, **colic**, etc. <note>original: "Hydropisin, Icteritiam, Paralysin, Colicam"</note>, and the man does as well, the **sovereignty** <term>Monarchy</term> <note>In Paracelsian medicine, a "Monarchy" refers to a self-contained system of natural laws governing a specific part of creation.</note> over the man is one thing, and over the woman it is another. She also differs from the man by as much as she was taken out of him—that is, by as much as she lacks in weight. She is from the man, but she is different; therefore, her **anatomy**, **philosophy**, **physics**, and **theory** <note>original: "Anatomi, Philosophia, Physica, Theorica"</note> are also different, and yet equal to the other two, for she is the smallest and

<column-break/>

and the hindermost world. And as far as the **womb** <term>Matrix</term> <note>The "Matrix" refers here to both the biological womb and the generative essence of the woman as a "mother" of life.</note> teaches you to recognize and distinguish the woman from the man, so far shall you also separate her diseases from those of the man. And consider that what is not possible for the world is possible for the man, and what is impossible for the man is possible for the woman. Therefore, since these things are separate, all their diseases are separate as well.

As our eyes now give us the task of the body—to undertake a different **philosophy of medicine** for the woman—we should not compare her diseases to those of the man, nor rely solely on the similarity of symptoms. For although death is the same in men and in women, the man is one thing and the woman is another. Even if the physician recognizes a disease in both men and women, and though death and misery are the same, and thirst and hunger are the same in both, the thirst of a man is different from that of a woman, and so too is the hunger. For when a man hungers, the "middle world" <note>The Microcosm, or the human male.</note> hungers; when the woman hungers, the "last world" <note>The woman, as the final and most refined stage of creation.</note> hungers. For the last is different from the first or the middle; as far as a mother is separated from her son, so far do these things stand from one another. Therefore, it is not fitting to look upon the origins of the ancients...

# The Book of the / **WOMB** <note>original: "MATRICIS"</note> 70
**S ii**

...sovereign writers, who however lead others astray by treating women and men as equal in their diseases, such as **paralysis** <note>original: "Paralysis"</note>, **apoplexy** <note>original: "Apoplexia"</note>, **epilepsy** <note>original: "Caducus"; literally "the falling sickness"</note>, etc. But this is false: the root of the woman is one thing, that of the man is another; therefore, medicine is also [different] in the understanding of the causes and in the knowledge of the cure. Although it eventually becomes clear that **menstruation** <note>original: "Menstrum"</note> and the like are conditions specifically distinct from the man—a distinction even a peasant is capable of making, knowing what a woman is that he is not—the physician does not wish it so. Rather, it is the deceiver of medicine and the deceiver of the sick [who refuses the distinction], because if the physician does not say: "a man's **apoplexy** arises thus from this or that **subject** <note>In Paracelsian terms, the 'subject' refers to the underlying physical or spiritual entity being acted upon.</note>, which is created and given only to the man," and "a woman's **apoplexy** comes forth from its root, the **womb** <term>Matrix|The womb, which Paracelsus viewed as a microcosm of the world within the woman.</term>, which lies thus and so in her **subject**," then that physician is no physician at all. For there are two types of medicine on Earth: one for women and one for men. The woman’s is one thing, the man’s is another. A woman’s medicines are good for her, and a man’s for him. But he who does not possess this distinction treats his art as if water were the same for everyone.

<column-break/>

<insert>VB</insert>
   Thus is the foundation of medicine: one cannot apply it to both creatures, except with the mouth [in words], but not in practice. Why does the world recognize that one herb is feminine and another is masculine? It recognizes this because the diseases are also distinct. If it were all one thing, why would Nature need to be divided in its medicine? It is divided because two worlds exist within humanity: the woman and the man. Because they are not equal in their diseases, medicine is therefore divided. <insert>VB</insert> See there the fraud, and how medicine has stood in error until now. Therefore, derive the prescriptions for men from their medicine, and for women from their **anatomy** <note>original: "Anatomi"; here referring not just to physical structure but to the essential nature of the body.</note>. # The Book of the / **WOMB** <note>original: "Das Buch der MATRICIS"</note>
**S ii**

Air is one food for humans; the Heavens <note>original: "Himmel"; in this context, the sphere of fire or the firmament</note> are the second; the Earth the third; and Water the fourth. These four foods a human must have daily, and cannot exist without them. From these, the human is sustained, for from them grows his nourishment and everything he requires. Now, once a human is conceived, he is within the **womb** <term>Matrix|The womb, which Paracelsus describes as a distinct "microcosm" or world within the woman.</term>. Now, the seed <note>original: "Sam"</note> of the human is the human himself; therefore, he must also have food, as befits a human. However, he is not in the outer world, but in the "last" world <note>the internal, protected environment of the womb</note>; therefore, the outer world does not feed him, but the inner one does. For this reason, the inner world must nourish him. This is the cause why the woman is the world, much like a mother, but the man is not; for this reason, you [the man] are no mother. Because the human does not grow and is not partitioned within the [outer] world, but within the smallest world—which is the world that he becomes—that same world must also give the human his nourishment until he enters the great world. Because the **womb** is thus that very same world—containing Heaven and Earth, Air and Water, which nourishes the human from his birth <gloss>into the world</gloss>—the woman is therefore something else in her entire body than the man. For the nourishment of children comes out of her, not out of the man. God has made two, and made the one to be a world, to enclose the seed

# The Book of the
## MATRIX.
<note>original: "MATRICIS," the genitive form of the Latin "Matrix," meaning "of the womb."</note>

Things are separated; even more separated are those things that belong even more to **Physics** <term>Physica|Here referring to the natural philosophy or the essential physical laws governing a body.</term>. Since the woman is a different [being], she stands upon another root; that root is the **Matrix** <term>Matrix|In Paracelsian medicine, the womb is not just an organ, but the "root" or internal heaven of the woman, governing her entire health and nature.</term>; she is created for its sake. Since she is created for the sake of the Matrix, she is also established upon it—that for which she is created and from which she takes her name. Thus she must exist out of it, and she must also have all her nature, condition, and the like from it. This distinguishes Woman and Man: that the Man grows as a Man, in a masculine way, and the Woman [grows] in a feminine way; that is, she is maintained and governed by the Matrix. From this it follows that all her illnesses also come from it—that is, they are born out of it along with the root. In this they [men and women] separate from one another: that all illnesses of women are naturally conditioned and characterized by the womb <note>original: "Mutter condition," referring to the uterus or "mother-organ."</note>, but the Man is not; rather, [he is] of a masculine nature. By this one sees how far one is from the other—for instance, the **Paralysis** of women and that of men. And although it is true that the same signs <note>original: "zeichen," meaning symptoms or diagnostic markers.</note> occur, is it not reasonable? Is the woman not a human? Yes. The man also? Yes. Is it not reasonable that they resemble each other as humans, but with the **Physics** and the specific nature of the **Monarchy** <term>Monarchy|A Paracelsian term for a sovereign domain of nature; here, the separate biological systems that govern male and female health.</term> reserved [as distinct]? Should that not be a

<column-break/>

distinction—that a woman is physically called a Matrix, and *is* Matrix, and the man is not? And that all her illnesses are of the Matrix? For she cannot have masculine elements; she is a woman and is a Matrix. And although the names of women's diseases may be unified and named and called the same as those of men, it is nevertheless nothing other than this: they are both called humans and are humans, but with the distinction stated above. But it does not follow—just because the same signs are encountered and both are humans—that therefore both should be of one nature, or their two essences be one essence. That does not follow; rather, the distinction must be maintained forever: that the Woman stands upon the Matrix, and grows upon it and has grown [from it], and that the Matrix is her root. Therefore, it is the first thing that the physician should consider in her illnesses, for they grow out of it—and not out of masculine powers, but out of **Matrix-powers**. From these you must derive the **Physics**, the **Cause** <note>original: "Causā"</note>, and the **Indications** <note>original: "Indicia"</note>; otherwise, all that one does is in vain. another and are within one another, so also is the human being in the woman. The woman is nothing else than like the sea in which many fish are. But because he has his soul, he is born, which is not the case for the fish; thus, just as one thing belongs to the fish and another to the water, and one to the water and another to the fish.

<margin>Physics.</margin> No "I prove" <note>original: "probo"</note> stands with them, nor with you, nor with me; it stands in * **Philosophy**, in the foundation of the **Light of Nature**; in that same light, all things should be tested, and not by the chatterers and babblers, who, as mentioned above, have built upon their own mad heads away from their true ground and foundation, so that they might deceive whoever hears their voice.

What shall I say of nature, but that she is invisible, and no one sees her first **Matrix** <term>Matrix|In this context, the womb or the primordial nurturing environment from which all life emerges. It refers both to the biological uterus and the spiritual/elemental source of the cosmos.</term>? For who can see that which existed before him? We all come out of the Matrix, yet no one has seen it, for it existed before Man. And although Man comes out of it, and is born from it continually, yet no one has seen it. The World is born out of the Matrix; Man is also; thus, whatever creature exists is born out of the Matrix. Therefore, it is necessary to describe what the Matrix is. Now, the Matrix is that from which Man grows; and for that reason, all that exists in the four **Elements** must be invisible there. Just as the World is the Matrix of all growing things, so too is the Matrix in the body, which must be regarded with the same **Anatomy**. Before Heaven and

# OF THE MATRIX.
<note>original title: "MATRICIS," the Latin genitive form meaning "of the womb."</note>

Earth were created, the Spirit of God hovered upon the water and was carried above it; this water was the Matrix. Within the waters, Heaven and Earth were created, and in no other Matrix. In it, the Spirit of God was carried—that is, the Spirit of God which is in Man, and which all other creatures do not have. For the sake of this Spirit, so that it would not be alone, Man was created in its image, with the Spirit of the Heart within him. Therefore, the Spirit of God dwells in Man; it is from God and returns to Him again. Since the world was then nothing but a water, and the Spirit of the Heart was upon the water, that water became the Matrix of the world, and within it all further creations. Now, it was all still a Matrix for Man, in which God created a dwelling for His Spirit within the flesh; the Matrix of those humans was the whole world, and his seed was the **Limbus** <term>Limbus|A Paracelsian term for the "great chaos" or the primordial "dust" containing all the potential of the universe from which Man was extracted.</term>, a seed in which the entire world stood. This is the origin of the first Man. Furthermore, Man was separated from this same Matrix and made his own Matrix out of himself—that is, woman—who henceforth is nothing less than the entire world. And the Spirit of the Lord is in her, which forms and places itself within her fruit.

<meta>This page continues Paracelsus's critique of traditional medical theories regarding the "Matrix" (the womb and female reproductive system). Following the previous page’s argument that menstruation is merely a waste product rather than a "bloom," he now addresses the misconception that this fluid nourishes the fetus or becomes breast milk.</meta>

->### THE BOOK OF THE MATRIX###<-

Thus all waste products <note>original: "Excrement"; in this context, it refers to any metabolic waste or bodily discharge.</note> stand still until winter comes, with its frost, its snow, etc. Then, the waste products flow again. Here one must understand the **time of birthing** <term>tempus pariendi|The specific period or season of delivery</term>, what kind of **condition** and **proportion** <note>The physical and temporal requirements for birth.</note> it has; the physician should speak of these things. For if he does not know them, everything he says is in vain. It is a blind assertion to say that the **menstrual flow** <term>Menstrum|Menstruation</term> should provide milk and be the nourishment of the child. O you dreamers <note>original: "fantasten"; refers to those who follow imagination rather than the observed laws of nature.</note>, investigate nature better before you write! These things are explained better in **Philosophy**, where the generation of man is written.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Therefore, know here the **anatomy of the matrix** <term>Anatomiam Matricis|The structure and spiritual nature of the womb.</term>—which is the entire body of the woman—that the child within the mother has its nourishment from the breasts outside the mother; from the breasts comes the best, most noble milk, not from waste products which they call **menstruation**. No poison on earth can equal that fluid; it is more harmful and more severe, yet you claim it should be a food for the child. How crude and unrefined is the talk that a human being [is sustained] by such poison—for there is nothing more harmful anywhere than the **waste products** in a woman and others when they are released—and it undergoes no further transformation into good things. That is to say: no poison becomes a food, but remains a poison and not a food. What father is there who would give his child a stone instead of bread,

<column-break/>

or a snake? None. God even less so; consider that. But it is true that nature stands still in its poison and is held back, just as the sun was held back in the days of Joshua <note>A biblical reference to Joshua 10:12, where the sun miraculously stood still; Paracelsus uses this as an analogy for how natural cycles pause during pregnancy.</note>. Thus also all the flow of nature stands still, from the time of conception until the child's need to transform and be nourished like a human ceases. It is also true that at birth the body cleanses itself of what should not be there; that is, what you think becomes milk. Rather, a **false growth** <note>original: "Wasserkalb"; literally a "water-calf," referring to a uterine mole or a failed, fleshy conception.</note> would sooner come from it. It is Divine faithfulness that such filth is shielded from the child to prevent harm. And yet you say the child is nourished by it? No thing has ever been nourished by filth, but by pure things, be they trees, herbs, roots, etc. Everything is nourished and sustained by subtle **liquids, dew, rains** <note>original: "liquoribus, rore, pluuiis"</note>, etc. Above all else, the child requires such nourishment, for the child is more important than the herb in the field and is the most delicate of things. Therefore, it is entrusted to its mother and her skill until its strength is present, by means of the most subtle foods. What else is that same flow in a tree but its own **seed** <term>Sperma|The vital life-force or reproductive essence.</term>? All things cleanse themselves through foam; so also does nature cleanse itself through foam, so that the child's food may become pure. And yet you say this foam is a food, that **menstruation** is the

...milk, and remains in the **womb** <term>Matrix|The uterus or the entire female reproductive system</term> until the child is born. Then it becomes milk; here one clearly perceives with what kind of **Philosophy** <note>original: "Philoſophia"; for Paracelsus, this refers to the study of the invisible forces and inner workings of nature.</note> you have been washed! That which rises—take careful note—is the foam from the cooking, which the child has consumed in the mother's womb and must suck from the breast. Now see how the **waste products** <note>original: "Excrement"; here meaning bodily discharges or metabolic byproducts.</note> are purged. Do you not have enough understanding? If one wanted to skim the foam from a drink, nothing would remain in the end. These are the **austere Stoics** <note>original: "Stoici auſteri"; likely a jab at rigid traditional scholars who focus on external logic rather than the lived reality of natural transformation.</note>, etc.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Therefore, it is now proper to present women’s diseases and health in a separate **Monarchy** <term>Monarchey|A distinct medical domain or biological kingdom governed by its own laws.</term>, since she is so widely separated from men—not only because of the breasts, the womb, and **menstruation** <term>Menstruum|The menstrual cycle</term>, but also because of the whole body, which is created for the sake of the breasts, the womb, and menstruation. Therefore, if the body is created for her sake, it is created according to her nature and not according to a foreign nature—that is, according to the male nature. And if perhaps a medicine helped women and men equally, as in the **plague** <note>original: "Peſte"</note>, cold pains, etc., know that in those things it only happens in a **narcotic** manner, a **stupefying** <note>original: "Stupefaurender"</note> manner, or a **diaphoretic** <term>Diaphoretiſcher|Sweat-inducing treatments used to expel toxins from the body.</term> manner, etc. These do not exist from the true origin of ordered medicine, because they do not come from the right...

<column-break/>

...right order. For that reason, it does not help this morning in the plague, nor in other [illnesses], of which more will be mentioned in its own chapter, etc. Since the right order should now be used, it is now proper to separate women with their health and their diseases from men, and to take this example: A woman is like the **tree** that bears its fruit, and the man is like the **fruit** that the tree bears. Understand it thus: the tree must have much to maintain itself so that it may give that for which it exists. Now see how many ailments may befall the tree and how few the pears. Just so much more is the woman above the man; the man is to her like a pear to its tree, which falls, but leaves the tree standing. Now the tree takes care again for other fruits, for its longer life; therefore it must have much, suffer much, and bear much for the sake of its fruit, so that it may come forth well and happily. Therefore, weigh this carefully if you wish to be **physicians** <note>original: "Arzt"</note>, what the example contains and how it is to be understood; otherwise, from your <del>no</del> <gloss>art</gloss> <note>original: "kunſt"; Paracelsus uses "art" to mean the learned skill and spiritual insight of a physician.</note> will never come a good physician. Therefore, look at the **Monarchy** separately, wherein you see what is a special necessity for the tree that is not necessary for the pears, and what is necessary for the pears that is not necessary for the tree.

<summary>Paracelsus argues that women's medicine must be a distinct "Monarchy" because the female body is fundamentally different from the male. He uses the analogy of a tree (woman) and its fruit (man/child) to show that the source of life is more complex and prone to illness than the fruit it produces.</summary>
<keywords>womb, matrix, female anatomy, tree and fruit analogy, medical monarchy, menstruation, Paracelsian philosophy, medical education</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Paracelsus’s argument that women require a separate medical category. He moves from the previous discussion on breastfeeding and the womb to the ontological difference between the sexes, based on his interpretation of the creation story in Genesis.</meta>

->## THE BOOK OF THE MATRIX ##<-

Just as there is an outward sign, so it is with men and women as well: by this, one knows the difference between boys and girls as easily as one understands the difference between a **pear** and its **seed** <note>original: "birn / vñ sein kern"; Paracelsus uses this analogy to show that while they come from the same source, their internal "core" or essence is distinct.</note>. Just as these two types differ from one another, so too are boys and girls distinct; I entrust the further study of this to **Philosophy** <note>original: "Philosophey"; in this context, the study of the inner nature of things.</note>. Now, consider within yourselves how Man is created from the **Limbo** <term>Limbo|In Paracelsian thought, the *Limbus* refers to the primordial "clay" or "extract of the world" from which Adam was formed.</term>, but Woman is not. She is the second creature, not the first, nor was she made at the same time as the first; therefore, she is subject to the Man. Since she is the second creature and not made from the Limbo, she possesses a different body. For if she were the same body as Adam, she too would have been made from that same Limbo. However, because she is meant to govern a different **Monarchy** <term>Monarchey|A distinct biological or medical domain governed by its own natural laws.</term>, she was subsequently made from living flesh—flesh that already existed. And yet, from that flesh, a different kind of flesh was made, just as flesh was made from the Limbo which had not existed before it was formed. Thus, the woman is made from the man's flesh, but she did not remain the same substance; rather, she is as far removed from the man as the man is from the **clay** <note>original: "leimen"; the mud or earth from which Adam was shaped.</note> from which he was made. This provides the reason to establish a new **Theory** <note>original: "Theoricam"</note> for women and to separate them into their own unique **Medicine** <term>Physica|The study of natural science and the physical body.</term>.
<meta>fica/</meta>

<column-break/>

This proves those to be liars who claim that women and men are the same, even though they appear to be so on the surface—excepting, of course, the matter in which the child is placed and the necessary functions related to it. Now, look at these same necessities: she must have a whole body for this purpose, and there is not a single drop of blood in her body that is not essential to the **Matrix** <term>Matrix|The womb or the entire female reproductive system.</term>. Therefore, it would be unjust to treat them as the same; but the old doctors did not understand this, and the young ones do not want to understand it. Indeed, it makes things easy and pleasant for you to say, "**Galen** <note>Galen of Pergamon (129–216 AD), a Greek physician whose theories dominated Western medicine for over a millennium.</note> says this," or "**Avicenna** <note>Ibn Sina or Avicenna (c. 980–1037), a Persian physician whose 'Canon of Medicine' was a standard textbook.</note> says that," and so on. By doing so, you keep your hands clean. You think you have hit the mark simply because they said it, as if that makes it true. First, put your author to the test to see if he is truthful, and then examine him; but that will prove too difficult for you. As long as one places faith in **lazy authors** and holds their words as dearly as the **Gospel**, and since you are no more pious than they are, you rightly remain stuck with such lying and fictional authors. For "like attracts like," and the Devil always herds them together. Just as there is a special nature and Monarchy in women, there must also be two distinct Monarchies: one for women and another for men. From this follows a different knowledge of the **Heavens** and of both **Spheres** that exist within: that of the **Microcosm** <note>original: "Microcosmica"; the "small world" of the human body, which Paracelsus believed mirrored the "Macrocosm" of the universe.</note>.
<sig>X</sig> <meta>ca</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus argues that women require a unique branch of medicine because they are "second creatures" made from flesh rather than primordial clay, making them as different from men as men are from mud. He criticizes physicians who blindly follow Galen and Avicenna, insisting that the female body is entirely dedicated to the function of the womb.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the argument that medical treatment must differ between men and women because their underlying physical substances—their "corpora"—are fundamentally different, even when they present with the same symptoms.</meta>

# THE BOOK #

...consensus, which has been abandoned by the "blind" doctors mentioned before.

Since the human body differs from one to another—one in the woman, another in the man—a distinction must briefly be presented here regarding their illnesses. So that the aforementioned indication may be more easily understood, observe this example: Imagine a case of **jaundice** <note>original: "Geelsucht"</note> in a man, and one in a woman. Both cases of jaundice have the same **signs** <note>original: "Signa"; clinical symptoms</note> and **prognoses** <note>original: "Prognostica"; the predicted course of the disease</note>. Judging by their value, they would be considered one **genus** or **species**; to all appearances and indications, they are not different species in the man versus the woman, but rather one species of disease—that is, they are one and the same thing. From this, one might conclude there is one **cure** <note>original: "Cura"</note>, which is to say, one medicine. But in truth, that cure will not work. I will explain the reason to you: although there are **hermaphroditic medicines** <term>medicamina Hermaphroditica|Medicines considered "universal" or "neutral" that could theoretically be used for both sexes.</term> which serve both sides, it is necessary to make a special distinction here (which I will set aside for now). According to the requirements of the correct order, understand it thus: The woman has jaundice just like the man, and yet more besides. That is, the **body** <note>original: "Corpus"; here meaning the underlying material substance of the person</note> that the jaundice inhabits is the **flux** <term>Profluuium|From the Latin for "flowing forth"; in this context, it refers to the specialized fluid systems and reproductive environment of the womb.</term>. What is the flux? It is not the **waste** <note>original: "Excrement"</note>, which is to say the **menses** <note>original: "Menstrum"</note>, but rather

<meta>catchword: rather</meta>

<column-break/>

# [OF THE] MATRIX #
## X ij ##

rather, it is the entire body from which the menses proceeds. That same body retains the name **flux** when it becomes a disease; it is then the **liquid of the microcosm** <term>liquor Microcosmi|The essential bodily fluids that Paracelsus believed mirrored the waters of the greater world (the Macrocosm).</term>. This liquid is subject to jaundice, which occurs in this manner: there is one cause in the man and in the woman, but not one **body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note>. In the same way, if you dye a **cloth** yellow and you dye a piece of **wood** yellow, that is one color—one disease—but two different bodies. Now, because there are two bodies, even though there is only one color and one sign, the medicine must be formulated for the **body** and not for the color.

<margin>Wood plane</margin>
In the same way that wood is mastered with a **plane** <note>original: "Hübel" / "Hobel"</note> and iron with a **hammer**, one must pay attention to these things: the **body** must lie before the doctor’s eyes. If he does not take this into account, he will not drive the disease out. Even if he arrives with a medicine that takes away the color, the body remains diseased. For when the **liquid of the microcosm** enters its flux, it becomes white. What is that then? It is the **white sickness** <note>original: "Weisse sucht"; historically known as Leucorrhea or "the whites."</note>. The other is yellow. The white does not come out of the yellow unless there is a cessation; just as many illnesses result in red, white, yellow, or black, all of which belong under one chapter. Since the **body** must be addressed for itself, and not the colors, but rather...

<margin>Plantain for <unclear>flux</unclear> <note>original: "Plantago ad diff..."; likely referring to *difficultas intestinorum* or dysentery</note></margin>
...but rather the **body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note> and colors; thus, there must nevertheless be a divided medicine. And if you already make do with hermaphroditic **simples** <term>simplicibus|Single-ingredient herbal remedies, as opposed to compounds.</term>, you will find your answer in its proper place: that you act not out of skill, but out of folly and lack of understanding. For it is to be understood in no other way than this: you simply take whatever you find, one after another—if it hits the mark, you have it. That is the "art" of women's medicine, not men's medicine. No men's medicine should be given to a woman, and no women's medicine to a man; each **monarchy** <note>original: "Monarchey"; Paracelsus's term for the distinct biological and spiritual "kingdoms" of the male and female systems</note> must be kept within its own **anatomy** <note>original: "Anatomi"</note> and not mixed. Whatever is mixed performs a work on both sides that is never truly completed.

For every hermaphroditic medicine <term>Hermaphroditica|Remedies traditionally thought to work for both sexes.</term> should be given as it is, and not **composed** <note>original: "componiert"; meaning mixed into a complex formula</note>—that is the first point. **Plantain** <note>original: "Plantago"</note> stills the **flux** <note>original: "ruhr"; dysentery or severe diarrhea</note> in men, and in women as well; it is **hermaphroditic**. Yet even so, it divides itself; it contains both **anatomies** within it, unlike other medicines (of which there are not many). If it is used for a man, then the **Arcanum** <term>Arcanum|The secret, essential healing virtue or "soul" of a medicine.</term> of the woman dies away; if it is used for the woman, the other side dies away. It is from these hermaphroditic medicines that **experiments** <note>original: "Experimēt"; empirical, trial-and-error remedies</note> have arisen—that is, "this for that," whether in men or women. Therefore, in other **simples**, no "experiment" stands, but only the 

<column-break/>

**Practical Canon** <note>original: "Canonica Practica"; a treatment based on the established laws and rules of medicine</note>. But the reason that **hermaphroditic experiments** are considered sufficient is not because they are ideal—for why do these **experiments** help? They help when a disease does not arrive **canonically** <note>original: "Canonice"; following the standard rules of nature</note>, but rather against the **Canon**. In those cases they help. But whatever arrives **canonically** should be treated **canonically**; no **experiment** helps there. Likewise, if something does not arrive **canonically**, only an **experiment** helps, and no **canonical cure** <note>original: "Canonica Cura"</note> will suffice. 

There is much to be maintained regarding this knowledge, but it belongs in its primary place. Know also how the **writers** <note>original: "Scribenten"</note> so often place the **canonical cure** <note>original: "Curam Canonicam"</note> first, because they believe all diseases originate **canonically**. This is an error, and the **canonical cure** that follows is based on that same error. Then, when the **canonical cure** will not help, the **"Treasure of the Poor"** <note>original: "Thesaurus pauperum"; a famous medieval medical manual for those who could not afford physicians</note> is used afterward. There are many such pieces; "Item, use this," he says, "it has often helped." "Item, this is good," says another, and so on. Thus the error is obvious: they do not understand the distinction between a **canonical cure** and **experiments**. Look at these blind doctors!

Further, regarding the example presented—of **jaundice** <note>original: "Gelesucht"</note> or other similar diseases—know that there is not just one "yellow," but many yellows; many also are the types of yellow-

...searches: And although the **bile** dries out the colors, there is not just one color in it, but all yellow colors, as is presented in its specific chapter. Now, the yellow of women’s **bile** <note>original: "Galle"</note> is one thing, and the yellow of men’s is another; thus, the colors are also different. Therefore, because they are divided, the medicine is also divided. **Alsa** <note>A Paracelsian term likely referring to a specific mineral or salt-based extract.</note> is a finder of color; **Resselba** <note>A Paracelsian term referring to a specific transformative property or essence.</note> is a taker of color; **Centaury** <note>original: "Centaurea"</note> is a residue. Those primary components, however, which are found in things and are not **hermaphroditic** <term>Hermaphroditisch|Substances that supposedly work equally for both sexes.</term>, nor merely color-takers or experimental, must be addressed at their foundation so that the **body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note> is properly handled. For bodies are transformed in **canonical diseases** <term>Canonischen morbis|Diseases governed by the fundamental laws of nature and the "Matrix" rather than external accidents.</term> like a thing that is dyed and never fades, but where body and color remain inseparable within it.

When the matter becomes like this, the medicine must not be as stated above, but must come from its own **monarchy** <note>original: "Monarchey"; Paracelsus’s term for the distinct biological and spiritual "kingdoms" of the male and female systems.</note> which takes the body unto itself; and in the restoration of the body, health is also brought back, and thus the sickness departs. For there are two types in all sickness: according to the body and for the body. Although this distinction has not yet been maintained, this is the error: they have wandered in their cure, because there is a different bile in women, and yet it is still bile

<column-break/>

with all the properties that a bile should have. Thus, this same bile must serve in her **canonical diseases** through its own monarchy—that is, those same medicines which are ordered differently from men's medicines in another **anatomy** and **physics**. Therefore, what **male centaury** <note>original: "Centaurea mascula"</note> does for men, **female centaury** <note>original: "Centaurea fœminea"</note> does to the same degree for women. For God regarded this so highly that He gave women their own special monarchy; and just as He separated her from man into a different body and being, so He also set apart her world and gave her her own benefits and necessities. Over this He established the Physician to recognize these things—but not **Avicenna** or **Galen** <note>The dominant authorities in medieval medicine, whom Paracelsus famously rejected in favor of direct observation.</note>. For divine providence has anticipated such invented **intellects** <note>original: "Ingenijs"</note> and said that He Himself created the Physician; which is to say, "the Physician I have created can do it; the other who sets himself up is false." Now the proof is seen in the **light of nature** <term>Liecht der natur|Intuitive knowledge gained through observation of the natural world, a core Paracelsian concept.</term> where the false and the righteous are revealed.

Since all growing things are now split because of the two monarchies, as has been mentioned, the **firmament** <note>original: "Firmament"; the heavens or atmospheric influence</note>, the earth, the water, and the air are also split into these two monarchies, each to maintain its own properties.

<summary>Paracelsus argues that because men and women belong to different "monarchies," medicines like Centaury must be gender-specific (male vs. female varieties) to effectively treat "canonical" diseases. He rejects traditional authorities like Galen, claiming true medical knowledge comes from the "Light of Nature."</summary>

<meta>This page continues Paracelsus’s discussion on the fundamental differences between the male and female biological systems, which he calls "monarchies." He previously explained that while both sexes have similar organs, their underlying "anatomies" and spiritual essences differ, requiring distinct medical approaches.</meta>

# THE BOOK #
-> **OF THE MATRIX. 89** <-

Know then this: that the food a person eats and the "food" of medicine are not one and the same; that is, they are not of one essence and one kind. This is because all food that is eaten is simple. And even though humanity is split into the two **monarchies** <note>original: "Monarchey"; Paracelsus’s term for the distinct biological and spiritual "kingdoms" of the male and female systems.</note>, this split concerns only the medicinal powers and not the food or nourishment. Although food is more useful to women in many things from their own monarchy than from the monarchy of men, this occurs because of the afflictions which the body carries with it, and not because of the nourishment itself. A smith and preparer resides in the stomach; he makes human flesh out of it. Now, he has two ways about him, and he is a kitchen: one way is that if he is in a man, he makes manly flesh from it; if he is in a woman, he makes womanly flesh from it. The food is one and is human food, but it is the **Archeus** <term>Archeus|The "inner alchemist" or vital force that directs the body's functions, particularly digestion and healing.</term> who prepares it for the person within their monarchy. Therefore, the physician need not worry about prescribing nourishment; rather, he must ensure that medicine is directed into its proper monarchy. For whatever nourishment is, the **Archeus** masters it in the stomach and makes of it what belongs to him—just like a smith who can make whatever he wishes out of his iron, so it is with this one as well. But medicine is not like this; it must be given according to its 
<meta>catchword: Monarchy</meta>

<column-break/>

# THE MATRIX. 89 #

**monarchy**, so that it may be kept in its essence. For in that place [the stomach], the **Archeus** cannot change it into anything else than what it is. All the food that a person eats, once we have eaten it, he [the Archeus] makes into that which it is—that is, flesh and blood—for we are made of those. But we are not made of medicine; medicine is for that which we lack, according to our needs. It must be given to us from that same **species and genus** <note>original: "Specie vnd Genere"</note>, for the stomach does not make out of it what we need unless it has received from us what it requires; otherwise, it remains in its own power and the stomach expels it again. Therefore, the body and its diseases are two things, not one. Because of this, the **regimen** <term>Regimen|A prescribed course of medical treatment, diet, or lifestyle intended to restore health.</term> must also be split according to its monarchy. For a **regimen** should not be given to create blood and flesh, but rather to drive out that which has corrupted and poisoned the blood and flesh. Thus, there is a medicine and a food; that is, the **regimen** creates blood and flesh—not taken from nourishment, but from medicine. That is to say, the **regimen** should be both a nourishment and a medicine. For the body that is sick does not gain weight but rather wastes away. Therefore, if one wishes for it to gain, the **regimen** must be a medicine that...
<meta>catchword: to be=</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus distinguishes between common food, which the "Archeus" (inner alchemist) transforms into gender-specific flesh, and medicine, which must be specifically prepared for either the male or female "monarchy" because the body cannot transform it.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the two "monarchies" (the male and female biological systems). The previous page established that while the stomach (the "inner smith") processes food into flesh based on the person's sex, medicine must be carefully directed to the specific monarchy to be effective.</meta>

# THE BOOK #
-> **OF THE MATRIX. 89** <-

...bring it about that the body receives the food for its nourishment. Therefore, the **monarchy** <note>original: "Monarchey"; Paracelsus’s term for the distinct biological and spiritual "kingdoms" of the male and female systems.</note> must be maintained in the sick through both food and medicine, as far as the illness extends. But where the body is healthy, this is not necessary. It is true, however, that we do not generally place things that are both medicine and food—such as **lettuce**, **beet**, and **turnips** <note>original: "Lactuca, Beta, Rapæ"</note>—into the category of the monarchy. Instead, we must view them specifically, for they carry a medicinal power within them whose strength must be distinguished: in the monarchy of women, their **Bleta** <note>likely referring to the specific nutritional essence or "beet-like" quality suited for the female system</note>, and for men, theirs.

For from such an oversight, it follows that the firmament and the **astral course** <term>astral course|The movement and influence of celestial bodies, which Paracelsus believed governed the timing and nature of diseases.</term> intervene and cause a rupture among opposing forces. This person then lives outside of their monarchy, so that while the woman's cycle is regular, the man's system demands another kind of flesh. This leads the man toward the good in one way, while leading the woman toward evil in another, and other such things. To establish a **regimen** <note>a prescribed diet or lifestyle</note> for a healthy person is a greater task than establishing one for the sick to keep them healthy. Health is an extensive knowledge that applies to all limbs; whereas for the sick, the regimen is the least concern, but the **Arcana** <term>Arcana|The hidden, essential healing powers or "secrets" contained within natural substances.</term> are the most important. He who prescribes a **diet** for the healthy in such a state splits the

<column-break/>

**monarchy** and considers the course of the heavens and the **subject** <note>the individual patient</note> of the persons. Only then can he anticipate what is in the course of the food, for food has its own **star** <note>original: "Astrum"; the inner spiritual essence or celestial influence of a substance</note> just like the heavens; therefore, it resists the same. He who lacks this knowledge errs, for he must not only provide for what is happening now, but for what is to come. Now, the art lies solely in knowing what is to come, so that he may provide for it. The **physician** should possess this knowledge in abundance; if not, his art is but a killing and a murder.

Thus, while the **first matter** <term>first matter|In alchemy, the "Prima Materia" or the raw, fundamental substance before it is transformed.</term> of human flesh and blood is one thing—that is, food and drink—the **final matter** <term>final matter|The "Ultima Materia," or the finished, specific substance (like male or female tissue) after the body has processed it.</term> consists of two things that follow from that one first matter: namely, man’s flesh and woman’s flesh. These are as distinct from one another as meat and fish. Yet, neither the first nor the final matter is to be considered alone. For God, who created man and woman together, made them as "one flesh." That is, they are to eat and drink one food, and not two. Therefore, they share one **first matter**. But the **Archeus** <term>Archeus|The "inner alchemist" or vital force that directs the body's functions, particularly digestion and healing.</term> is different; he directs what he eats into his own monarchy. Though they belong together and are to be fed from a single pot, the results are distinct.

<summary>This page explains that while men and women share the same "first matter" (food), their internal "Archeus" transforms this food into distinct "final matters" (male and female flesh). Paracelsus emphasizes that a physician must understand how astral influences and medicinal foods affect these two separate biological "monarchies."</summary>

So, since a **theory** <note>original: "Theorica"</note> is necessary here, understand
it thus: That there is a need to know what is being 
**transformed** <note>original: "transmutiert"</note> and how that happens; also 
what the **Archeus** <term>Archeus|In Paracelsus’s view, the "inner alchemist" or vital force that directs the body's functions and digestion.</term> possesses within itself, so that out of 
that which is **One**, it makes **male flesh** and 
**female flesh** <note>original: "Menstrisch fleisch"; referring to the distinct biological nature of the female body.</note>—which are a great distance 
apart from one another. This is what must be 
undertaken here: Since diseases come and are 
born out of food, it is necessary to recognize 
this at the source—but in the **final matter** <note>original: "Vltima materia"</note>, 
not in the **first matter** <note>original: "Prima materia"</note>. This is the case unless 
the **first matter** were **hermaphroditic** <note>possessing both male and female qualities</note> in its 
powers, or if there were a medicine with a 
**divided monarchy** <note>original: "gespaltener Monarchey"</note> concerning the man or 
the woman; if such is set aside and taken away, 
the disease is likewise taken away. But if 
the defect were in the **final matter** and not in 
the **first**, then it will not be found in the **first**,

things, so it is also in others that touch upon and encounter disease. If such a nature were in the womb, would it not require a different **Theory** <note>original: "Theorica"</note>? But furthermore, in the bearing of children, there is another factor: a good seed makes a good fruit. Thus, the seed and the tree are two separate things in this place, divided into two parts. The tree of the earth produces its fruit continuously without the seed, but the tree of the woman does not. Rather, the seed is placed into the tree—that is, by the man. From this it follows that much depends on the seed; namely, if the seed is worth nothing, the tree cannot make it good. Therefore, whatever affects or concerns the tree also encounters the seed; they must both be good. And if they are both good, then only one good thing results: the fruit.

Therefore, there are other accidents that befall the **Matrix** <term>Matrix|In Paracelsian medicine, the womb is seen as the fertile soil or "matrix" where life and disease are cultivated.</term> arising from the planting and sowing of the seed. Now, however, the woman's **Matrix** is divided between her own specific diseases and those diseases which she catches and receives from men.

This is a rightly fashioned **Theory** <note>original: "Theorica"</note> which proceeds from the light of Nature and not from imagined heads. The diseases of women that come from the man require masculine medicine. From this, it followed that the "men’s medicine" for the **green sickness** <note>original: "für das grün"; likely referring to chlorosis, a form of anemia traditionally called the "green sickness."</note> also drove away the "green" in women. What was the cause? It was not that the disease itself was the cause, as they believe, but rather that she inherited it from the man—that is why the medicine helped. However, what she has from her own nature cannot be helped in such a way; rather, she must be treated through her own **Surgery** <note>original: "Chirurgey"; here referring to the specific internal "craft" or mechanical operations of the female anatomy.</note>, and through no other way. Therefore, a medicine helped sometimes and sometimes not, for the reasons presented above. This applies not only to one disease, but to all of them. For you must know this: if the seed is afflicted with **Jaundice** <note>original: "Geelsucht"</note>, that same seed in the womb causes **Jaundice** in the woman. and impression, then **Smothering** <note>original: "Suffocatio"</note> arises from it, which is identical to the **Falling Sickness** <note>original: "Caduco"; traditionally referring to epilepsy</note>. **-na** <note>original: "Arcana"</note> healthy. An **Arcanum** <note>original: "Arcanū"; a powerful, secret spiritual essence of a remedy that works directly on the soul of the disease</note> is no virtue, but a **force** <note>original: "vis"</note> and **power** <note>original: "potentia"</note>, more than a virtue. Although the error has long resided in medicine that they have called these "potential forces" and "virtues"; therefore they did not understand Hippocrates, nor his commentators. With this, I wish to have generally concluded the **Monarchy** <note>original: "Monarchey"; here meaning the specific realm or sovereign biological system</note> that belongs to women, to be considered by the physician. Although I do not wish to have ended it here, if God wills, but rather to describe further every sickness of women—both those they have in common with men and those they have alone without the commonality of men—I shall describe each one so that this Monarchy of the Microcosm may be understood and recognized, with an exposition and explanation of all those things and causes which necessity requires, with masculine, anatomical, and **Archimic** <note>original: "Archimischen"; likely referring to the Alchemical or Spagyric art of preparing medicines</note> instruction, along with the origin of **mundane** <note>original: "Mundanischē"</note> instruction, outside of which no one can be a **Physician** <note>original: "Medicus"</note>. And although it is true that in this Monarchy I separate myself entirely from other physicians, it is only right; because the same ones are found sufficient in their ignorance and lack of foundation to heal, and they are accustomed to write without the **Light of Nature** <note>original: "Liecht der natur"; the divine wisdom revealed through the observation of the natural world</note>. Yet a physician should write nothing unless it be in the light of nature, just as he writes. For if he is to take the medicine from the Earth, then the Earth must...

<sig>Dd</sig>
<meta>catchword: the</meta>

<summary>This page explains that healing occurs through the same "virtues" and celestial influences that cause disease, and introduces "Arcana" as a more potent healing force than mere virtue. The author also asserts that a true physician must understand the "Monarchy" of womanhood and work within the "Light of Nature."</summary>
<keywords>Microcosm, Womb, Matrix, Hippocrates, Arcanum, Light of Nature, Alchemical medicine, Celestial influence</keywords>

--- Page 110 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument that medical knowledge must be rooted in the natural world rather than just intellectual theory. Paracelsus is critiquing the contemporary medical education system, arguing that true healing comes from the Earth and God, not from the established "pillars" of the medieval university.</meta>

# The Book /

<page-num>105</page-num>
<header>The Book /</header>

The doctor must be a physician <note>original: "Artzt"</note> and not merely a man; he must also take his instruction from the Earth, so that he knows how to use it, for the Earth gives to where it belongs. If he does not practice and know this, then his writing and <margin>☛</margin> undertaking are nothing but a deception to the physician and his patients. Although it is true that they are highly dedicated and established as one of the four pillars of the university <note>original: "Hohenschule"; Paracelsus often mocked the traditional "four pillars" of academic medicine—Philosophy, Astronomy, Alchemy, and Virtue—when they were taught without practical experience</note>, upon which they place great importance, as if no one should speak against such a status. Yet know this: regarding these things, those who established them admit themselves that they ordained them for that reason, or they do not know whether their theorizing is correct or not. They let their deeds be their own defense; they were only prompted to it by certain experiments and reasons <note>original: "rationes"</note>. But whether that is the true foundation, they let it be their own defense. That is setting a poor pillar; it has a poor foundation. Furthermore, they build much upon this foundation and say that those who established the universities to be the "fourth limb" do not err. In truth, there has been a wild error in the way you have been established. They took their foundation from Christ, who says that the sick have need of a physician. So you have kept the name, and they intended that you should be just that. But truly, had they better considered that God created the physician and his medicine out of the Earth, and that he should be given his place, they would have let a question circulate in the school

<column-break/>

<page-num>106.</page-num>
<header>OF THE WOMB.</header>

wondering whether you were created by God or by the Devil; that is, whether you have dealt in truth or in lies. Then it would have been clearly tasted who had created you. For God did not create the damned schoolmaster, legal agent <note>original: "Procurator"</note>, apothecary, priest, monk, and the like to be a physician. Leipzig, Tübingen, Vienna, and Ingolstadt <note>These were the major prestigious universities of the Holy Roman Empire at the time</note> created you; thus you are just like the creators who worked there. It is no less true that you taste something in astronomy <note>original: "Astronomi"</note>, something in philosophy <note>original: "Philosophey"</note>, and something in logic <note>original: "Logica"</note>; but what you taste is neither cold nor warm. If the astronomer put away his divination <note>original: "Sortilegiũ"; refers to superstitious fortune-telling rather than the study of celestial influences on health</note>, and the philosopher his irrationalities <note>original: "irrationabilia"</note>, and the logician his lies, then it would be well that you had a foundation in medicine. That you wish to defend yourselves with Machaon, Apollo, Aristotle, Galen, Averroes, Avicenna, Rhazes, and Mesue <note>These represent the "canon" of ancient Greek and medieval Arabic medical authorities which Paracelsus famously rejected in favor of direct observation</note> is far off the mark; you must first prove whether they have lied or not. If it is found to be so, then it will be permitted or not. If you were the pipes of a written work, it would have to be a good organist who could play a little song for me on them. It is the same with them as with the geometricians <note>original: "Geometrischen"</note>, who speculate on strange circles and instruments that drive themselves and move; and in those, they fly with their instruments.
<sig>DD ij</sig>
<meta>catchword: Die</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus delivers a scathing critique of the medical establishment, arguing that true doctors must learn from the Earth (nature) rather than university dogma. He lists several major universities and classical authorities, claiming their teachings are built on "poor foundations" and irrationality.</summary>
<keywords>medical education, university critique, Leipzig, Tübingen, Vienna, Ingolstadt, medical authorities, Galen, Avicenna, philosophy, astronomy, logic</keywords>

--- Page 111 ---
<meta>This page continues Paracelsus’s sharp critique of academic physicians. Picking up from the previous page's discussion of the biblical justification for doctors (Christ’s statement that the sick need a physician), he now argues that his contemporaries use this divine mandate merely as a "wooden horse"—a hollow facade—to hide their lack of true healing power and their greed.</meta>

# The Book /

The [wooden] horses [you provide] are made of wood/ and when it comes to the actual riding/ then it is all trickery <note>original: "gaugelwerck"; refers to juggling, sleight of hand, or deceptive performances</note>. If you did not have God as an excuse/ and used Him to sell your foolish ways/ you would be found far cruder/ than any alchemist or dowsing prophet <note>original: "Wünſchel Prophet"; refers to one who uses a divining rod to find water or ore, often viewed by Paracelsus as a figure of questionable legitimacy compared to true natural science</note>: But you say/ God does not will it/ God has done it/ who would dare to interfere with His judgment or power/ all things are in His hand: Why do you speak this way? Because you are of a wicked nature/ therefore the *Word of the Lord* <note>original: "Verbum Domini"</note> sits in your mouth/ and you leave the rest out: The sick have need of their physician <note>original: "Artzt"</note>/ why? This you do not say: Even if you do say it/ it is only said/ so that people give you money/ and so that they believe you/ but the heart of the physician is far from the tongue. Therefore consider
->this well/ do not leave it un-<-
->answered.<-

->The End of this Book.<-

<column-break/>

<page-num>2</page-num>

<summary>In this concluding passage, Paracelsus accuses academic doctors of using religious platitudes to mask their medical incompetence and greed, comparing their static theories to 'wooden horses' that cannot actually ride.</summary>
<keywords>physician, hypocrisy, Word of the Lord, trickery, dowsing, greed, medical ethics</keywords>
